## SR-054 — Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-054 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-054
- **Title**: Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)
- **SN-Refs**: SN-024;SN-023
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The state view the delivered generators produce shall support its core reading tasks with low friction: a first-time reviewer finds the project state, the next work, and how the parts connect, each within one tab switch; views default to a legible density (start-collapsed per the greater-than-3 rule) and reveal detail without losing context; labels stay readable at default zoom with no clipped or overlapping text.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — task-level usability is perceptual (is this findable, is this legible), so a test can confirm an element exists and not that a reader can use it. Acceptance is adjudicated against a written rubric derived from the stakeholder intent rather than from the test, because a test written by the builder encodes the builder's own idea of usable.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A fresh family-heterogeneous CRITIQUE session judging the generated dashboard against docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md (authored at [v3]-[g2]) returns APPROVE citing numbered anchors. The RUBRIC is the acceptance instrument and the single home for which anchors are live: an anchor whose core has been bound to a test is retired there, and a verdict must not cite a retired anchor. This row therefore does not enumerate the live set - it would be a third copy of it, and the copy that goes stale. A clause of the requirement above that a test now holds is verified through the LLR/TC chain, which the registry already records, not by a verdict.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 3
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-055 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-055
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Dashboard usability rendering
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_views.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_html/_nav/when_view
- **Detail**: Adjudicated against the usability rubric (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md) by the LLR-048/LLR-082 critique loop: labels stay readable at default zoom with no clipped or overlapping text, and the reading experience the rubric's remaining anchors describe. Byte-deterministic; --check unchanged. This row keeps ONLY what a test cannot pin: the mechanized cores have left for child rows that name the tests owning them — T2 start-collapse (LLR-099/TC-102), T3 breadcrumb-restores-parent (LLR-100/TC-103, the whole anchor, no residue here) and T1 labelled entry (LLR-115/TC-120, the whole anchor, no residue here). Do NOT restate a child's claim here: an anchor lives in exactly one row.
- **Rationale**: A critique that re-judges what a test already enforces spends its budget twice and can disagree with the harness, so an anchor whose bar becomes operational leaves this row entirely rather than being kept as a duplicate. T3 and T1 are the two that left whole: a critique cannot verify T3's return path from a static shot set while a test can click the crumb, and T1's 'obvious' was always glossed as labelled-not-unlabelled, which is mechanizable once written that way.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-055)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-099 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-099
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Default-density start-collapse (T2 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: know_view/sw_view/_render_drill
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): a view whose scale EARNS tiering — a bundle spanning more than 3 OKF types, a module set above the component threshold (the LLR-052 '>3' rule) — renders START-COLLAPSED as the shared type-tiered drill, one block per type or container, rather than a flat wall of nodes; at or below the threshold it stays flat. Covers the Knowledge, How-SW and landing What views; the What icicle start-collapses to one block per SN with descend-on-click, reusing _drill_layer_svg/_render_drill so the interaction idiom is single-sourced. SCOPE: T2's remaining 'reads legibly at default density' judgement stays perceptual under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: Tiering imposed on a small project costs a click to reach what already fitted on one screen, so it is earned by scale rather than switched on globally. Capping DEPTH instead of collapsing was rejected and the emitter records why: height is leaf-proportional, so stopping at the SR lane still stacks one unit per SR and the wall returns. The What icicle is the case that forced the widening — it rendered the whole spine at LEAF scale, one unit per test case.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-102)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-100 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-100
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Detail-in-context breadcrumb (T3 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _render_drill/DRILL_SCRIPT
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T3 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): descending a container never replaces the view with no way back — the drill emits a declared breadcrumb nav (<nav class="crumbs"> with a data-root-crumb root label) whose crumb click TRUNCATES the trail to that ancestor, restoring the parent view. SCOPE: the WHOLE anchor — the breadcrumb's presence AND its restore behaviour; T3 is retired as a critique anchor and no residue is left under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: A drill that descends with no way back strands the reader in a view they cannot leave, and 'keeps the reader oriented' never independently passed or failed a round — the breadcrumb IS that orientation, so the claim becomes testable only once it is the thing asserted. Leaving the anchor to critique was rejected because a critique CANNOT verify it: a shot matrix captures initial views only, so a round can pass on markup while the return path itself stays unverified, whereas TC-103 clicks the crumb. It follows that a critic finding T3 violated has found a gap in TC-103, which routes through change-intake to harden this test rather than through a verdict.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-103)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-105 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-105
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Interactive-control contrast in both themes (T5 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _ring_ink/_ring_style/_cedge_marker
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T5 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): EVERY control painted over a node's own fill clears the WCAG 3:1 UI-boundary floor against EVERY node fill declared in STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS, in BOTH themes — computed per fill (white or near-black, whichever contrasts more), never a single fixed hue. Covers the descend/hover/focus ring AND the containment/descend arrow (.cedge shaft plus its marker head), which derives from the same per-fill --ring token; its marker head takes a per-ink marker because marker content is rendered from the defs tree and cannot see the referencing node's custom properties (a constraint recorded in _arrow_markers), and RING_INKS is the closed ink set that makes that emission total. SCOPE: a control's contrast against its host fill, arithmetic like the sibling A4 tests. Whether the affordance otherwise reads as inviting stays perceptual under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: A fixed hue vanishes on whichever fill happens to match it, and the failure is total where it lands: the measured collision put a control at 1.00:1 against its host, i.e. invisible. Fixing only the ring was rejected once the same fixed token was found on the descend ARROW — the very control a reader must find to descend a tier — measuring 1.06:1 light and 1.99:1 dark on a shipped phase fill. One control's fix does not generalise; deriving every such control from the host fill does.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-108)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-115 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-115
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Task findability - labelled entry within one tab switch (T1 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
- **CodeSymbol**: build_html/_next_work_html/_nav
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Each of the three core reading tasks the rubric concretizes — find the project state, find the next work, find how the parts connect — is reachable within one tab switch of the landing view, its entry point a LABELLED nav control or a NAMED surface on the landing view, never an unlabelled region a reader must discover by clicking. THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT that makes the check honest: it is judged against the RENDERED artifact carrying real registry data, NEVER the nav skeleton. The guard resolves each task's entry point from the emitted document (task 1 -> the landing Definition-completeness/Execution cards, 0 switches; task 2 -> the landing Next-work card, 0 switches; task 3 -> the How-SW nav tab, 1 switch), asserts each is LABELLED and within one switch, and asserts the next-work surface is NON-EMPTY on the zero-active/all-queued fixture. SCOPE — PROVEN: a labelled entry point per task, reachable in one switch, and the next-work surface non-empty across the practice fixtures (zero-active, waiting-with-predecessor, drained). DELIBERATELY NOT: whether the reader subjectively knows what to do next; T1 leaves the critique entirely, with no residue under LLR-055/TC-055.
- **Rationale**: 'Obvious' was always glossed as labelled-rather-than-unlabelled, and that gloss is the whole bar — once it is written operationally there is nothing perceptual left to adjudicate, so the anchor can leave the critique instead of being re-judged every round. The rendered-artifact constraint is the load-bearing part and must not be relaxed to a structural check: a next-work surface can EXIST in the emitter and render nothing when no row is active, so a nav-skeleton check passes on exactly the artifact a reader finds empty.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-120)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-116 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-116
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Viewport-fit by scale-to-fit with a legibility floor (T7 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _svg_fit_style/SHRINK_FLOOR
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T7 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Every emitted SVG (all three wrappers: _svg_wrap for dag/sw/know, the icicle panel, _drill_layer_svg) carries width:100% + max-width:<natural> + min-width:<natural x SHRINK_FLOOR> instead of a FIXED pixel width, so a diagram fills its container and scales down as the viewport narrows. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowing: a view whose natural width exceeds 390 / SHRINK_FLOOR still scrolls horizontally WITH its cue — the row's own fallback rule made mechanical. Closing that remainder needs a narrow-width re-layout (stacked columns), which is not this row.
- **Rationale**: A fixed pixel width pins the rendered size whatever the container does, which is why every narrow view demanded sideways scroll and one graph clipped a component label mid-word. A viewBox alone cannot fix it — the fixed width still wins. The legibility floor exists because pure scale-to-fit TRADES this anchor for T4: a 900px graph squeezed into 390px shrinks a label past readable, so the floor stops the fix from creating the defect it is next to.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-121)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-117 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-117
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: Theme-lock: one theme mechanism and no mixed-family surface/ink pair (T6 core)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: HTML_TEMPLATE theme tokens + every emitter's paint
- **Detail**: The mechanized core of usability anchor T6 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). The measurable content is FAMILY PAIRING, not the absence of literals: a fixed status swatch carrying fixed white ink is theme-locked CORRECTLY, since neither side flips. A page inverts mid-view in exactly two ways and this row forbids both. (1) A SECOND THEME MECHANISM below :root — the document declares exactly one prefers-color-scheme block, targeting :root, and one color-scheme, also at :root. (2) A MIXED PAIR — a theme-varying ink on a theme-invariant surface, or the reverse. The two families are DERIVED from the emitted document (the dark block's override set IS the definition of theme-varying), never hand-listed, and the dark block may not override a token :root never declares. Applied to CSS rules declaring both a background and an ink, to every emitted SVG node pairing an inline rect fill with an inline text fill, and to CSS-declared SVG text fills paired against the family their host nodes actually paint in. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: --ring is excluded because it is stamped FROM the host fill (LLR-105 owns its floor); the JS-composed badge idiom pairs background and ink at runtime from the invariant vocabulary, where LLR-114's arithmetic owns it; and the page CSS has exactly ONE rule declaring a classifiable background AND ink today (body), so that half is prospective while the sweep's weight sits on the node and text-fill halves. Whether the chosen palettes are PLEASANT stays perceptual.
- **Rationale**: A per-component theme override is a seam the reader crosses while scrolling, and a mixed pair reads correctly in the theme it was authored in and inverts in the other — both are invisible to the author working in one theme. Forbidding colour LITERALS was the obvious rule and is the wrong one: it fails a correct fixed-on-fixed pairing and passes a varying-on-invariant one, so it would flag the safe case and miss the defect. Deriving the families from the emitted document rather than listing them means a new token joins a family by existing.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-122)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-119 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-119
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: T4 label legibility, mechanized: ink inside its box, truncation with a reveal
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _drill_block_label/_fit_lines/_next_work_title
- **Detail**: The mechanized floor of usability anchor T4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md), widening the anchor's existing owner rather than adding a rubric anchor. The clause made objective: no character a block renders may fall outside that block's own rect, horizontally or vertically. The emitter FITS the sub-label to the column it will actually be drawn in — a second line when one will not hold it, then an ellipsis — with three text lines the ceiling, reusing the grid the id/name wrap idiom already proved fits row_h; a sub-label that already fits one line renders byte-identically to before. SCOPE — PROVEN: for every block of every drill layer in the emitted document, each label line's ink box lies inside its own node rect, measured from the emitted geometry with the emitter's declared per-character widths and the document's declared font sizes, entities counted as the single glyph they render. Second half: this emitter does not truncate the next-work title by a fixed budget — the card emits the whole clause and lets its own width do the fitting, the declared bound _NEXT_WORK_TITLE caps ONE item's HEIGHT rather than its text, and where it bites the remainder discloses through a NATIVE <details> cut at a word so head and remainder rejoin continuously when opened. DELIBERATELY NOT: whether an ellipsis a reader meets is ACTIONABLE — truncated-without-affordance stays T4's perceptual residue under LLR-055/TC-055, so T4 remains a live critique anchor; the full string keeps its homes in the block's title, aria-label and data-summary.
- **Rationale**: A label is illegible AS a label the moment its ink leaves the box it names, which is the half of this anchor a reader cannot argue with. Column right-sizing (LLR-057) cannot absorb it: right-sizing clamps at MAX_TIER_COL, so a 400-character need asks for a 2000px column and gets 172 — the overflow is structural, not a tuning problem. A FIXED character budget was the shipped approach on the next-work card and is rejected: it spends the same budget at every width, cutting a title mid-word on a wide screen with the card half empty. Native <details> is the point of the reveal — operable by pointer AND keyboard with no script, in a file opened from disk, which is what makes the affordance assertable from the markup instead of needing a browser. The height bound is measured over 320 registry rows: median 44, p90 126, max 609 characters of leading clause.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-124)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-120 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-120
- **SR-Refs**: SR-054
- **Title**: No edge through an unrelated node box (T8 objective floor)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_graph.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _route_edges/_detour_d/_polyline_hits
- **Detail**: The mechanized floor of usability anchor T8 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). T8 is two clauses and this row claims only the first: NO EDGE PASSES THROUGH AN UNRELATED NODE BOX. SCOPE — PROVEN: across the shipped dashboard plus one fixture per emitter, every emitted wire, sampled as the polyline a reader's eye follows, clears every node rect that is not its own source or target. MEASURED at the binding: 264 shipped wires, 0 through a box. DELIBERATELY NOT: crossing count, lane separation, or where a crossing falls — that residue stays under LLR-055/TC-055, which is why T8 remains a live critique anchor. Separately and NOT claimed here: at the router's own 3px clearance rather than the bare box, one wire does graze, so _detour_d exhausting its lane candidates and returning the least-bad fallback is a reachable state rather than a theoretical one; that wire's identity and the lane-separation work belong to the open work item.
- **Rationale**: A wire crossing an unrelated box reads as a connection that is not there, which is a wrong answer rather than an untidy one — geometry a reader cannot argue with. The claim about the ARTIFACT is deliberately separate from the router's own unit tests and is why this row exists: _detour_d returns a LEAST-BAD fallback when no candidate lane clears, so 'the algorithm detours' does not imply 'nothing shipped through a box', and the artifact-level claim had only ever been hand-verified once.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-125)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-070 — Offline project-state core

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-070 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-070
- **Title**: Offline project-state core
- **SN-Refs**: SN-010;SN-023;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered generator set shall produce the adopted repository's self-contained, offline, deterministic and freshness-checkable project-state views from the tracked registries alone.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-010 and the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check, by isolating one independently reviewable obligation formerly carried by SR-038.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The root state view renders its registry-derived views (completeness tiles, the SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC hierarchy, the WI roadmap DAG) without external fetches and remains usable offline; regeneration from unchanged registries is stable; each generated view carries a --check freshness contract.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-039 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-039
- **SR-Refs**: SR-070
- **Title**: OKF bundle emitter
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_okf.py
- **CodeSymbol**: emit/concept/_doc_title_and_summary/on_disk
- **Detail**: emit() builds {relpath: content} for every real row (sorted, deterministic, no timestamps): frontmatter via JSON-quoted YAML scalars, bodies carrying the row prose plus up/down markdown links from locally built reverse maps, per-tier and root indexes, the UPSTREAM.md spec pin; on_disk() snapshots docs/okf for the byte-compare; write mode writes-if-changed and prunes; small loaders duplicated per the F5 rule (no new sibling import). Layer B2 adds a process-guides tier: _doc_title_and_summary derives each guide title+summary from the doc first heading/paragraph (inline links stripped so nothing dangles), emitted only after the vacuity gate so a fresh scaffold stays vacuous. Graph links resolve within the bundle; each Process Guide carries a resource pointer to its source doc, which is left unmodified. --check trips on a registry or doc edit, a missing bundle file and a hand-added extra, recovering after regeneration; docs/process.toml [checks] okf_export = false silences the step, with the legacy docs/okf-export sentinel still read through the SN-028 migration window; off and placeholder-only registries pass silently, and process guides emit only alongside a real spine.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-042)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 1

- **LLR-ID**: LLR-051
- **SR-Refs**: SR-070
- **Title**: Process reference tab
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py;project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: process_panel/build_html
- **Detail**: Renders the Process tab as a fully self-contained conditional panel registered in build_html (the Knowledge-tab pattern: nav button + section id=process): (1) the artifact-lifecycle x gates panel derived from the live registries + docs/gate (current-gate highlight; per-tier counts from the spine), (2) the resume-loop panel (the managed agent_loop flow with its escalation edges), (3) the slices -> phase -> gates panel derived from the WI registry (docs/work/); links out to the process masters wherever a canonical home exists (bounded in-view restatement only where no single doc states the relationship); sorted inputs, no clocks; a data-less repo renders byte-identically; --check unchanged (the trajectory-map step). Process-doc link-outs resolve; the tab is part of the adopted toolkit's generated project-state view (REL-002 output), not a system output.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-051)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 2

### LLR LLR-056 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-056
- **SR-Refs**: SR-070
- **Title**: Process tab intake + human-decision loop panels
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
- **CodeSymbol**: process_panel/_station_panel
- **Detail**: Extends LLR-051's process_panel with the project's two circular working loops as linked flow panels: (A) the intake loop - owner/agent intake -> triage into work items with spec detail -> the scheduler-derived ready frontier in the WI registry (docs/work/) -> build/review -> merge; (B) the human-decision loop - open-items population (incl. the gate-ratification table) -> human review and ruling -> the log's Decisions record -> merge - with the LLM_Agent entry node rendered once and shared by both loops. Each stage links to its canonical home (status.md, the WI registry (docs/work/), open-items.html, log.md); data-derived where a canonical source exists; a data-less repo renders byte-identically; --check unchanged (the trajectory-map step). Every stage link resolves to its canonical doc; generation is byte-deterministic; the loop panels are part of the adopted toolkit's generated project-state view (REL-002 output), not a system output.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-056)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 3

### LLR LLR-057 (current)

- **Title**: Decomposition render polish (columns, arrows, persistent hover)

- **Detail**: Polishes the tiered decomposition render: each tier column is sized no wider than its content within a declared bound (a per-tier max-width cap MAX_TIER_COL, not the current uniform column); one horizontal parent->child arrow is drawn per containment edge so containment is explicit rather than implied by nesting alone; and the hover/focus highlight persists on the last-hovered item until another takes it (a stateful highlight keyed to the last-hovered node id, killing the flash-on-exit). Self-contained, byte-deterministic; --check unchanged. Composes with LLR-052's tiering without changing its >3 thresholds. A data-less repo renders byte-identically; the polished decomposition views are part of the adopted toolkit's generated project-state view (REL-002 output), not a system output.

## SR-112 — Checked per-agent skill fan-out

## SR-114 — Kit scripts run across the supported OSes

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-114 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-114
- **Title**: Kit scripts run across the supported OSes
- **SN-Refs**: SN-003;SN-011
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The kit scripts shall run on Python 3.11+ across Linux, Windows and macOS.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-011 — a script that works only where its author ran it turns portability into a defect the adopter finds, and the path, line-ending and locking differences that break it are invisible on a single platform. Testing one platform and reading the others by inspection was rejected: every one of those differences has been a real failure here. The macOS exclusion argument: the 3.11 floor is a platform-agnostic language/stdlib guarantee already exercised on Linux and Windows, while macOS's job is OS-specific behavior (fcntl.flock, paths, git worktrees) that does not vary by Python version — a redundant-coverage call, not a runner-availability workaround (3.11 has arm64 macOS builds).
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The CI matrix spans Linux, Windows and macOS on Python 3.11 and latest (3.x) and is green; the macOS+3.11 cell is excluded as a redundant-coverage call recorded in the rationale; the hosted matrix as an evidence surface is SR-152's contract.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Analysis
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: portability

## SR-129 — Registry representation migration

- **SR-ID**: SR-129
- **Title**: Registry representation migration
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The kit shall provide a mechanical converter between the work-item registry's home - the docs/work/ spec folder (status = directory, TOML frontmatter, Deliverable in the body) - and the retired docs/requirements/work-items.csv form, refusing any status outside the known vocabulary, any materialization into a non-empty target, and any conversion while an in-flight active/<branch>/ claim exists (conversion is a drained-stop operation), with the round-trip provable over the live registry.
- **Rationale**: The 140-cell lesson: an unproven representation change is where a registry silently loses cells. The converter is the migration path every adopter of the folder home takes, and the single spec writer the filing seam reuses (concurrency-restructure spec, Phase 2).
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: wi_convert --verify over a populated registry exits 0 reporting cell-exact equality; an unknown status refuses naming the row; folder->CSV->folder is parse-equal including a hand-filed spec with no order key; an in-flight claim refuses conversion by name.

- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Modified
- **Phase**: 4

Refuses unknown statuses by row, non-empty targets without --force, and any in-flight active/<branch>/ claim by name (status is the FIRST path component, the loaders' rule; conversion is a drained-stop operation per §3.2 - the Phase 4 claim protocol made the state reachable).

## SR-137 — One policy home, with a checked shape

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-137 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-137
- **Title**: One policy home, with a checked shape
- **SN-Refs**: SN-028
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-01;B-04
- **Requirement**: The kit shall read every process policy dial from a single docs/process.toml whose line shape is a checked contract (one key = value per line under a bare [section] header; no dotted keys, inline tables or multi-line strings), refusing — never resolving by precedence — a repo in which any dial is declared both there and in its legacy one-word file.
- **Rationale**: Two grammars read this file — `tomllib` in Python and the three git hooks in pure sh, so a Python-less box still fails closed on a declared privacy gate (M-42). TOML is far more expressive than a `grep -E` can follow, so every shape only one reader understands is a silent flip of a security gate; the first cut shipped five such shapes, three of them fail-OPEN. Narrowing the shape and CHECKING the narrowing is what makes one file safe for two readers. ONE HOME, settled 2026-08-15: the two-grammars-agree observable — the same answer from the hooks' sh and `tomllib` over the adversarial file-shape table — is SR-031's and SR-031's alone. Both rows claimed it and the texts had already DIVERGED, only SR-031's naming the trailing-comment decoy that once made the privacy gate fail OPEN, which is the whole reason the table exists. The partition that survives: SR-031 states the package-wide coherence property (every enforcer reads one value per dial, both grammars agreeing) at B-05; this row states the dial's HOME and its CHECKED SHAPE, and the refusals a session gets back, at B-01/B-04. What stays here is the shape check's own fail-safe direction — a shape only one reader understands is refused AND still reads ON in both — because that is a property of the narrowing, not of the comparison. CROSSING ATTRIBUTION EXAMINED AND CONFIRMED 2026-08-15 (the act-7 revision ["B-01","B-02"] -> ["B-01","B-04"], applied and flagged for overrule): read against external.toml, B-02 carries exactly three things - rulings, attestations and Status flips - and this row's shall contains none of them. Its subject is the dial file's HOME and SHAPE, so its observables are the config edit arriving as a governed write through the hook floor (B-01) and the refusal going back out naming the key and both files (B-04). The contrary reading - that declaring a policy dial is itself an authority act - was weighed and REJECTED on the partition it would destroy: the dial's MEANING is SR-139's and that row keeps B-02, so putting B-02 here would give one authority observable two homes, which is the defect this campaign exists to remove. One honest strain is recorded rather than hidden: B-04's `carries` names the hook floor and subagent_gate specifically, while this row's refusals also fire at the dispatcher preflight, intake's adjudication arm and the integrator's verdict gate - session-facing guardrail verdicts of the same class, but not the two the crossing enumerates. If that enumeration is meant as exhaustive, B-04's own text is what needs widening, not this row's attribution.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A dial declared in both homes produces a refusal naming the key and both files, at every guarded entry point (the dispatcher's pre-claim preflight, intake's adjudication arm, the integrator's verdict gate); a dotted key, inline table, multi-line string or hook-read key under the wrong section is refused by the shape check AND still reads ON in both readers; a wrong-typed or out-of-range dial is refused rather than defaulted.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-155 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-155
- **SR-Refs**: SR-137;SR-139
- **Title**: The config layer: one reader, checked shape, one comparison
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_common.py
- **CodeSymbol**: process_config/process_shape_findings/config_conflicts/ratification_level/human_holds
- **Detail**: `process_config` parses `docs/process.toml` once; `declared_policy` reads a dial through it with the legacy file as fallback; `process_shape_findings` refuses every line shape the hooks' sh cannot follow; `config_conflicts` returns refusal STRINGS (never raises — `dispatch.run` sits in the tick loop's caller, where an exception would rewrite an exit-code contract) covering the double-declaration, the wrong type and the out-of-range value; `ratification_level` + `human_holds` are the ordinal and the ONE comparison every consumer makes.
- **Rationale**: Five entry points read policy without passing through `agent_loop.main`, so the refusal has to live where the value is read. The comparison is stated once for the same reason the shape is checked: the failure modes here are silent, and a rule restated at four call sites is four chances to state it differently.
- **TestRefs**: TC-150;TC-151
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-137 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes

- **Rationale**: The hard mixed-config refusal in SR-137 is only safe to ship if a downstream adopter never meets it un-aided. ## SR-139 — Ratification as an ordinal over a derived spine stage

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-139 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-139
- **Title**: Ratification as an ordinal over a derived spine stage
- **SN-Refs**: SN-029
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-02;B-05
- **Requirement**: The kit shall express human ratification authority as a cumulative integer 0-4 naming the highest spine tier a human still ratifies, compared against a separately derived SPINE STAGE (0=SN..4=nothing in process) with a declared, auditable mapping to the harness gate — every unreadable or out-of-range input resolving toward MORE human involvement.
- **Rationale**: The retired three-value enum could not express "TCs are human-held but LLRs are not", and four tables each re-interpreted the same word with its own fail-safe direction. The failure that matters is a machine ratifying something a human meant to hold, so every degrade has to point the same way — including the ones that look like kindness: clamping an out-of-range level toward 0 reads as "nothing is human-held" and disarms every hold in the repo. CROSSING ATTRIBUTION EXAMINED AND CONFIRMED 2026-08-15 (the act-7 revision ["B-02"] -> ["B-02","B-05"], applied and flagged for overrule): B-02 is unarguable - the ratification level IS the authority input, and every fail-safe clause here describes how that input is read when it is broken. B-05 earns its place on a test, not on the fact that everything here is delivered content: the acceptance clause "Level N holds exactly the tiers the SHIPPED TEMPLATE documents" is a claim about the package's own contents agreeing with its behaviour, which is falsifiable against the package alone with no authority act performed - the same package-wide property class SR-031 occupies. The objection this had to survive is the measured one: B-05 already carries about four fifths of the layer's references, so adding a row to it needs an observable that is genuinely AT the package, not a default. This row has one. That the imbalance itself may be wrong is a separate question about the frame rather than about this row, and it is where it belongs, as OI-29.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Level N holds exactly the tiers the shipped template documents (cumulative, strictly-less-than the stage); stage 4 is held by no level, the end-of-run hold being its own dial; an unreadable stage, an absent dial, and a wrong-typed dial all read as human-held; an out-of-range level falls back to the conservative end AND is refused loudly; a legacy gate-policy word reads as all three dials it meant.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-155 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-155
- **SR-Refs**: SR-137;SR-139
- **Title**: The config layer: one reader, checked shape, one comparison
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_common.py
- **CodeSymbol**: process_config/process_shape_findings/config_conflicts/ratification_level/human_holds
- **Detail**: `process_config` parses `docs/process.toml` once; `declared_policy` reads a dial through it with the legacy file as fallback; `process_shape_findings` refuses every line shape the hooks' sh cannot follow; `config_conflicts` returns refusal STRINGS (never raises — `dispatch.run` sits in the tick loop's caller, where an exception would rewrite an exit-code contract) covering the double-declaration, the wrong type and the out-of-range value; `ratification_level` + `human_holds` are the ordinal and the ONE comparison every consumer makes.
- **Rationale**: Five entry points read policy without passing through `agent_loop.main`, so the refusal has to live where the value is read. The comparison is stated once for the same reason the shape is checked: the failure modes here are silent, and a rule restated at four call sites is four chances to state it differently.
- **TestRefs**: TC-150;TC-151
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-157 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-157
- **SR-Refs**: SR-139
- **Title**: The second axis: which tier is in process
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/derive_gate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: spine_stage/stage_to_gate/_decomposed_sr_ids
- **Detail**: `spine_stage` returns the LOWEST unfinished tier, where a MISSING artifact belongs to its own tier rather than to its parent (an SR with no LLR yet is stage 2, because what is being written is an LLR), `Modified` is the SR's own tier and is checked FIRST, and any other not-yet-Verified SR is checked LAST because a child in flight is the honest answer while it is in flight. `stage_to_gate` states the mapping to the harness gate once, deliberately lossy in one direction only. The value rides `docs/gate` as an APPENDED basis field.
- **Rationale**: Reading a missing child as the parent's tier made stages 2 and 3 unreachable during exactly the period they describe, leaving the axis unable to express the distinction it exists for. Appending rather than inserting the basis field is required: `check.py`'s basis regex depends on the existing field order.

## SR-140 — The attestation anchor lives on the artifact's own row

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-140 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-140
- **Title**: The attestation anchor lives on the artifact's own row
- **SN-Refs**: SN-029
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-02
- **Requirement**: The kit shall record each acceptance on the accepted artifact's own row - the
commit whose tree carries the text that was accepted, a digest of that row's
NORMATIVE cells, the transition accepted, and the acting reviewer that accepted
it, so an approval delegated to automation on a tier the declared ratification
level released is distinguishable BY QUERY from a human one - never in a second
registry keyed on the same artifact.
It shall report text that has moved away from its recorded anchor regardless of
any Status movement.
It shall treat an anchor written in the same commit as the text it anchors as an
error.
- **Rationale**: Attestation STATE is already the row's own Status - derive_gate computes the gate from those cells and reads no other source - so a separate ledger was a second home for a fact the row already carried, and three of its seven columns duplicated cells outright (Decision=ratified|meaning IS Status; superseded IS SupersededBy). What the ledger got right is the ANCHOR ITSELF: 'has this been ratified' was answered by walking git for the newest commit where a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - precisely the sanctioned path the staged-amendment guard deliberately ignores. Two cells are kept rather than one because they fail in DIFFERENT directions: the commit is what a human can review and what the before/after brief diffs against, while the digest is what survives a squash-merge, a rebase or a shallow clone, any of which makes a recorded sha unresolvable and would otherwise silence drift detection repo-wide at once. Owner ruling 2026-08-09, docs/repo-lock.md D-1. One-shall waiver (13v): record / report-drift / refuse-same-commit-stamp are one anti-tamper contract whose clauses deliberately fail in different directions; splitting would mint three rows for one (SN-029, B-02) pair against the 13p invariant, and a participle re-word would bury two refusal contracts a TC must target by name. Extended 2026-08-15, WITHOUT adding a fourth shall: SN-029's DELEGATED-APPROVAL RECORD — "each automated approval on a released tier appends a durable record (row, transition, acting reviewer, commit) distinguishable from a human approval by query — releasing a tier means the review is delegated, never skipped" — had ZERO textual occurrence in the SR layer while `orphans=0` held (neither `delegated` nor `acting reviewer` appeared in any of the 64 rows). Two of its four fields, the row and the commit, were already this row's; the transition and the acting reviewer were the missing half, and they belong HERE rather than on SR-139 because this row is the one that says what an acceptance record contains and where it lives, while SR-139 says which tiers are held. The query-distinguishability is the load-bearing part: a record that cannot separate a machine approval from a human one turns a released tier into an unauditable one, which is the failure the need exists to prevent.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: An artifact whose normative text has moved since its recorded anchor is reported whether or not its Status moved; an anchor that cannot be recomputed, or that names an artifact which is not a current row, is a finding rather than silence; an acceptance recorded with no acting reviewer, or one whose acting reviewer cannot be told apart from a human's by query, is a finding — releasing a tier delegates the review, it does not skip it; a commit that writes an anchor cell while also changing that row's normative cells is refused, because a re-stamp in the same breath as an amendment hides the amendment; and the same rules hold for a stakeholder need, which has no Status cell for the amendment machinery to watch.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-158 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-158
- **SR-Refs**: SR-140
- **Title**: The one comparison basis an amendment is measured by
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: split_changed_cells/spine_cell_class/_RATIFIED_TEXT/staged_spine_amendments
- **Detail**: RE-POINTED 2026-08-15 (log 2026-08-15h) onto the mechanism that SURVIVED. This row used to describe a DIGEST engine (normative_text/sn_normative_text/digest/current_digests); the last_approved snapshot deleted it whole, because an approval that records what it blessed by COPYING the registries needs no canonical text to hash, no separator that cannot occur in a cell and no second exclusion list. The obligation is unchanged and is discharged by split_changed_cells(csv_path, id_col, head, row) -> {"ratified": {cell: (before, after)}, "traced": {...}}: the id column and Status are excluded STRUCTURALLY - the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in would make every flip read as an amendment and every amendment invisible behind its own flip - and the remainder is split by spine_cell_class, the SAME classifier the amend-without-flip guard reads, so the two can never disagree about what normative means. It is PUBLIC for exactly one reason: baseline_snapshot.is_drifted reads it as the drift basis (LLR-173), so the snapshot comparison and the staged-amendment guard are one rule with one home rather than two rules that agree until they do not. _RATIFIED_TEXT names the two Status values whose row TEXT is ratified (Verified, Planned) and requires the SAME one on both sides, so prose rewritten under a Planned row is no longer invisible. It returns the before/after PAIRS a brief has to render anyway, which is why it replaced a hash rather than being wrapped by one. Needs are covered by the whole-file copy rather than by a per-need projection: the three need tables have different shapes, and a projection would have hashed the garbling.
- **Rationale**: A ledger that does not parse is not an anchor at all, and every reader then falls back silently to the git-history derivation it replaced. The copy-based baseline removes that failure mode rather than hardening it: the record is a file a human can open and `git diff`. What had to be kept is the DEFINITION of a change - which cells count, and which of them are ratified rather than merely traced - and that is one function, cited by both readers, rather than a hash whose inputs each reader re-derives.
- **TestRefs**: TC-153
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-173 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-173
- **SR-Refs**: SR-140
- **Title**: The last_approved snapshot: the record of what was blessed
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/baseline_snapshot.py
- **CodeSymbol**: copy_live/load_all/rows_for/exists/stamp/is_drifted/drifted_cells/unanchored_findings
- **Detail**: The approval RECORD, sibling to LLR-158's comparison basis: SR-140 says an acceptance is recorded on the accepted artifact's own row and that text moved away from it is reported regardless of Status movement, and this is where the recorded side lives. copy_live(root, seed=False) mirrors seven registries (the four spine tiers plus interfaces/external/components, which carry human-only approval and state cells) BYTE FOR BYTE into docs/archive/last_approved/, preserving repo-relative paths so spine_carrier.resolve/carriers/stem resolve under the snapshot root verbatim, carrier fallback included; the stale other-carrier file for a stem is deleted in the same act, or the next read raises "exists under BOTH carriers". Whole files rather than extracted rows is what makes "snapshot file == live file at the copy commit" DECIDABLE - check_trajectory.staged_snapshot_findings is the mirror invariant that fails a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live on one comparison - and it is also what makes the UNANCHORED rule decidable, because the copy keeps each row's own Status cell, so a live row claiming approval whose snapshot copy reads below it is an approval that never rode a copy. Three refusals are load-bearing and each is the opposite of a convenient degrade: copy_live REFUSES to CREATE the directory without seed=True, because the first snapshot blesses whatever text is in the tree and must ride the owner's reviewed signing commit (reachable only from `intake.py snapshot --seed`); load_all returns None for an absent directory and RAISES on a file that exists and will not parse, because {} and None are opposite claims and the empty one reads as "re-bless everything with no diff shown"; and rows_for is the ONE place the None sentinel is collapsed, so no caller invents its own `or {}`. is_drifted/drifted_cells delegate the cell comparison to LLR-158's split_changed_cells and arm on the RATIFIED half only. stamp() is advisory and derived FROM GIT rather than from a file, so it can never quietly become the ledger this mechanism replaced; every arm degrades to ("", ""). ADVISORY TODAY: unanchored_findings returns strings and joins no failure set - it is promoted to the integrity floor at migration step 7, and not before, because against a pre-seed or pre-rename snapshot it would red every row.
- **Rationale**: The baseline had to leave git history. The derivation it replaces walked the registry for the newest commit at which a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - and the D-9 ladder deletes the flip, so the walk returns HEAD and the diff is empty BY CONSTRUCTION, on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. A snapshot on disk is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file, which is the one thing a walk over the live file can never be. docs/archive/ is the placement rather than an accident: check_vocab.EXEMPT_GLOBS already exempts it, and the snapshot legitimately holds the PREVIOUS vocabulary, so anywhere else reds the vocabulary enforcer on every signing. The design's own argument for whole-file copies is that a human can open the record and `git diff` it - a record only a script can read is a record only a script can audit.
- **TestRefs**: TC-167
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-153 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-153
- **Verifies**: SR-140;LLR-158
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: RE-POINTED 2026-08-15 (log 2026-08-15h) off the deleted digest suite onto the tests that verify the surviving basis. Run the drift half of the baseline-snapshot suite: it DRIVES the premise rather than asserting it - test_the_amendment_seam_is_BLIND_to_an_amend_plus_flip shows the staged-amendment guard silent on a sanctioned amend+flip over a real two-commit git repo, which is why a baseline recorded at acceptance time is owed at all - and then pins exactly what a change is taken over. test_a_ratified_cell_moving_under_an_approved_row_is_DRIFT (a ratified cell MUST move it), test_a_TRACED_cell_moving_is_NOT_drift (the traced half must not), test_status_itself_is_never_the_amendment (the marker is excluded structurally, so a flip is neither an amendment nor a mask for one) and test_a_row_below_approval_can_never_be_drifted (no claim to fall from) are the four corners of the split. That the guard and the snapshot comparison agree is no longer a test at all but a property of the code: split_changed_cells is the ONE public basis both staged_spine_amendments and baseline_snapshot.is_drifted call, so there is no second answer to disagree with.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-140 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-158 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_baseline_snapshot.py
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

- **TC-ID**: TC-167
- **Verifies**: SR-140;LLR-173;IF-123;IF-128;IF-129
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the record half of the baseline-snapshot suite over real temp repos - the half TC-153 does not cover, which is drift. (a) VACUITY: with no snapshot directory every reader is vacuous by ABSENCE, load_all returns None rather than {}, and the mechanical flip is a no-op before the first signing. (b) THE BOOTSTRAP GUARD: copy_live REFUSES to create the directory without seed=True, and --seed is proven UNREACHABLE from every loop module, hook and check step, so the first snapshot can only ride the owner's signing commit. (c) THE COPY CONTRACT: the copy is byte-for-byte and preserves repo-relative paths, and a stale other-carrier file for the same stem is DELETED in the same act (or the next resolve raises "exists under BOTH carriers"). (d) UNANCHORED, BOTH DIRECTIONS: an approved row absent from the snapshot is reported, an approval whose snapshot copy reads BELOW approval is reported (the case that is only decidable because the copy keeps each row's own Status cell), a registry missing from an EXISTING snapshot is reported, and an unparseable snapshot RAISES rather than reading as empty. (e) THE MIRROR INVARIANT: a clean copy passes; a hand-edited snapshot and a partial copy each fail; the prose README is exempt. (f) The snapshot-directory constant has ONE value in both homes, and the `intake.py snapshot` CLI seeds then refreshes.

## SR-144 — Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-144 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-144
- **Title**: Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record
- **SN-Refs**: SN-027
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch, with one immutable per-close report naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split — refusing a report that declares neither a split nor an explicit deferral, and refusing a second close of the same event rather than overwriting the first.
- **Rationale**: Five successive dedup mechanisms leaked because each reconstructed the return event from a MUTABLE proxy; a document that never moves dissolves the class instead of mitigating it. The refusals are what make "immutable" a property rather than a claim. The split is a field because its absence merged rejected code onto trunk on 2026-08-03: silence about it is different from saying "I could not judge", and only silence is unactionable.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A partial close is clean under the real validator (the terminal Deliverable rule is exempt, because an early close leaves the spec's definition byte-identical by design); a terminal spec with no report is LOUD rather than silently un-disposed; a second close is refused and leaves no residue in the lane; a multi-spec close that refuses restores every report it wrote.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-161 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-161
- **SR-Refs**: SR-144
- **Title**: The close ritual and the disposition it owes
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
- **CodeSymbol**: close_partial/render_report/report_path/_restore/_split_lines
- **Detail**: `close_partial` writes the report FIRST (it is the event's identity), refuses when one already exists, and restores EVERY report this call wrote on any refusal — so a multi-spec lane cannot half-close. `intake._close_drafts` titles the disposition on the report PATH and nothing else, and says so LOUDLY when a terminal spec has no report rather than minting zero rows quietly. Every partial and cancelled close earns exactly one disposition row — a re-sweep mints none, a genuinely second close is a second report and so a second row, and a disposition row never mints a disposition for itself (refused at both ends); a complete close is spot-checked at the declared rate rather than gated; continuing partial work drafts a successor carrying explicit lineage that survives the id change, never re-opening a terminal row.
- **Rationale**: The path is the only token in the event's identity that cannot move. An earlier cut folded the report's `claimed_outcome` in as well, which re-read a MUTABLE field on every sweep, so one edit minted a second disposition for the same close — the starvation class returning through a new proxy.
- **TestRefs**: TC-156
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-167 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-167
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
- **Title**: The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
- **CodeSymbol**: compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
- **Detail**: The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
- **Rationale**: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).
- **TestRefs**: TC-161
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-156 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-156
- **Verifies**: SR-144;LLR-161
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the handback suite over real git worktrees: a partial close is clean under the REAL trajectory check (not a stub), a terminal spec with no report is loud, a second close is refused and leaves no residue, and the disposition is keyed on the report path so a re-sweep mints nothing.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-144 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-161 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_handback.py;tests/test_intake.py
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-161 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-161
- **Verifies**: LLR-167;IF-124;IF-127
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Drive the real loop against a fake agent CLI over throwaway git repos, one per ROUTED brief, and capture what the session was actually handed. Assert the composed brief carries each slot's real derivation (the lane's report verbatim, the closed spec, the commit facts; the live census joined to the TC row and to the obligation it covers), that no `{slot}` survives in the output, and that the typed verdict line the brief demands is written to the path the brief named and committed with the result trailer. Then drive the refusals: a clean close (no per-close report), a report with no typed `commit_range`, and a census that has come clean each fall back to the worker assignment with the reason PRINTED. Pin the discriminator against the ambiguity it exists for - two rows with an IDENTICAL SpecRef and different declared `Brief` cells compose differently - and pin the two unrouted briefs as unrouted, naming themselves in the refusal. The amendment arm covers the two seams cited above (added 2026-08-15, log 2026-08-15h): IF-124, the `last_approved` baseline read - the brief carries the snapshot as its anchor, only RATIFIED cells reach the judge, and with NO snapshot the brief HOLDS and says FIRST APPROVAL rather than fabricating an anchor; and IF-127, the lazily imported re-attestation MODEL, which is what makes the brief and the document the owner signs one traversal rather than two.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-146 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_adjudicate_brief.py
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-146 — Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-146 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-146
- **Title**: Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail
- **SN-Refs**: SN-005
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Every prompt the delivered loop launches shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.
- **Rationale**: Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.)
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: An unknown or unfilled slot is a refusal; a missing shipped template is a named preflight refusal rather than a first-session crash; the catalogue lists every shipped prompt with its digest and `--check` fails when stale; a line-ending difference is not a digest change; an operator override is named in the session record rather than only in the launch flags.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-162 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-162
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146
- **Title**: Prompts as loaded files, catalogued and fingerprinted
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/prompts.py
- **CodeSymbol**: load/fill/strict_check/digest/catalog_rows/preflight
- **Detail**: `load` reads a shipped template (or an operator override) and raises a named `PromptError`; `fill` refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; `digest` normalizes line endings before hashing; `preflight` turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. `gen_prompt_catalog.py` renders the catalogue with a `--check` freshness gate, and `agent_common.prompt_fingerprint` records the rendered result per session beside the template's own name.
- **Rationale**: Normalizing before hashing is not cosmetic: the same template checked out CRLF on Windows and LF on POSIX is the same prompt, and a telemetry field that said otherwise would report a model change on every clone.
- **TestRefs**: TC-157
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-163 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-163
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146
- **Title**: Argv arrays, and the adjudicator as a routed phase
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
- **CodeSymbol**: split_cmd/build_argv
- **Detail**: `split_cmd` recognises a JSON array ONLY when the value parses as a list, so no existing template can change meaning; a non-string token raises rather than being stringified. The prompt-transport decision reads the resulting TOKENS, so it is identical for both forms. Alongside it, `ADJUDICATE` joins `agent_route.WEIGHT_PHASES` and `agent_loop.DEFAULT_PHASE_TIER` with its own `route_intent` arm: cross-family like the reviewers, tier pinned by the row's measured estimate, and deliberately deaf to the implementer's escalation overrides.
- **Rationale**: A silent coercion in a registry is a launch that does not match its declaration. And an adjudicator inheriting the implementer's escalation would silently re-tier a judgement because a different row's build went badly.
- **TestRefs**: TC-157
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-164 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-164
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146
- **Title**: The generated prompt catalogue and its freshness gate
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/gen_prompt_catalog.py
- **CodeSymbol**: render/main
- **Detail**: `render` is PURE — it takes no arguments and reads only the shipped templates — so `--check` and the write path cannot disagree about what "current" means. `--check` compares on NORMALIZED line endings, for the same reason the digest is taken that way: a CRLF checkout is not a stale catalogue. The file is declared in `docs/stack.ini [generated]` as kind `promptcatalog`, so the trunk lane owns it and a work branch never commits it.
- **Rationale**: A generated artifact nobody re-generates is a document that lies, and a lying catalogue is worse than none: it invites the operator to conclude the wrong thing about the session they are debugging. Same contract `gen_arch_map --check` and `gen_skills_index --check` hold.
- **TestRefs**: TC-157
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-009
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-147 — One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-147 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-147
- **Title**: One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The kit shall carry the SN->SR->LLR->TC spine in ONE machine-parseable carrier rather than two (stakeholder needs as markdown prose tables, the other three tiers as CSV, as they were until the 2026-08-10 cutover), and shall provide a mechanical converter that proves the conversion cell-for-cell over the live registries BEFORE the authority flip - refusing, with a nonzero exit that names the row and the field, any conversion in which a cell does not survive the round-trip.
- **Rationale**: The two-carrier split has no recorded rationale and costs on both sides. Reading 32 need rows takes ~166 code lines across 14 functions in 8 modules - six bespoke scanners, two of them F5 twins pinned by nothing but a docstring, which have already drifted once and rendered a phantom root in the dashboard - while reading all 436 SR+LLR+TC rows takes csv.DictReader. CSV also cannot represent the cells that actually exist: five hold a literal pipe, 552 hold commas, the longest is 1,553 characters, and an embedded newline has no representation at all. A structured carrier turns three integrity rules into properties of the parse - a duplicate id becomes a decode error, a ref list becomes a typed array (retiring the split-on-whitespace rule that read `and` as an orphan), and an empty cell becomes an absent key, so `unset` and `set to empty` stop being the same value. The converter is the migration's evidence, not its convenience: SR-129's 140-cell lesson is that an unproven representation change is exactly where a registry silently loses cells. One-shall waiver (13v): the single-carrier claim and its converter-proof are one contract — the converter is the migration's evidence — and the converter's mechanics already decompose to this row's LLR chain; splitting would separate a claim from the proof that makes it checkable.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: migrate_carrier.py --check over the live registries exits 0 reporting the round-trip clean for every row of every tier; a cell that is dropped, truncated, re-typed or re-ordered is REFUSED with exit 1 and a finding naming the registry, the row id and the field; and the emitted text re-parses with tomllib.
- **Priority**: C
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: trajectory

### LLR LLR-165 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-165
- **SR-Refs**: SR-147
- **Title**: The spine carrier converter (markdown + CSV -> TOML)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/migrate_carrier.py
- **CodeSymbol**: convert/rows_to_toml/sn_to_toml/compare/toml_scalar
- **Detail**: convert() walks the four tiers and returns (findings, written_paths), so the caller decides whether a lossy conversion writes. rows_to_toml emits `[<table>.<ID>]` with the id PREFIX RETAINED and BARE - TOML allows `-` in a bare key - because ~6,400 hand-authored citations use the prefixed token and a numeric key buys no type change (TOML keys are always strings, and `001` and `1` are two legal DIFFERENT keys, which is a near-miss the prefixed form does not have). cell_to_value types by COLUMN, not by guessing: REF_COLS become arrays, INT_COLS become ints, an empty cell becomes an absent key, and everything else stays a string - this is a carrier change, not a schema change. toml_scalar emits multi-line basic strings for anything long or newline-bearing (tomllib is read-only by design, so the kit emits its own, as it already does in wi_convert.toml_string and bootstrap.set_process_key). sn_to_toml keeps the edge-case tier's own lifecycle/scenario/expected fields as THEMSELVES and writes kind= from the heading, retiring section-as-state. compare() re-reads what was emitted and diffs it against an {id: {key: text}} projection built from either carrier, so ONE comparison serves both instead of a second copy that drifts.
- **Rationale**: The round-trip check is the requirement, not a convenience - and a check that cannot fail would be the false green SN-008 forbids, which is why the loss detector is driven against real corruption classes rather than only against a clean run. Ref cells compare on the TOKEN LIST because the conversion re-joins them with the canonical separator and the original spacing was never part of the meaning; comparing raw text there would report a loss that is not one. The edge-case fold that traj_parse._sn_fields performs is a PRESENTATION rule the markdown table forced, so it stays in the exporters - baking it into the carrier would make the exporter's reading the only reading there is.
- **TestRefs**: TC-159
- **Status**: Planned
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-166 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-166
- **SR-Refs**: SR-147
- **Title**: The spine carrier's one reader, and the dual-home refusal
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/spine_carrier.py
- **CodeSymbol**: SPINE_TABLE/SPINE_COLUMN/rows_from_toml/rows_from_text/resolve/load
- **Detail**: PURE like trace_text (text in, rows out; no git, no argv) except resolve()'s existence check, because the dual-home refusal is the one rule that must not be duplicated. rows_from_toml presents rows under TODAY'S COLUMN NAMES via SPINE_COLUMN - the exact inverse of migrate_carrier.KEY - so the carrier change is data plus a loader rather than a sixteen-module rename; SPINE_TABLE is keyed on the ID COLUMN, not on a path, because a path carries a carrier suffix and therefore moves. An absent key stays absent rather than becoming "", and a decode error returns None rather than {}. resolve() RAISES when both carriers exist rather than picking by precedence, and load() raises on a carrier that exists and will not parse. carriers() returns BOTH paths so an applicability test spans the cutover.
- **Rationale**: None-vs-{} and absent-vs-empty are the same defect twice: {} says the registry had no rows, which for a baseline read means re-bless everything with no diff and for a gate means nothing to check, so a carrier that cannot be read must be ABSENT rather than empty. The dual-home REFUSAL follows the rule bootstrap --migrate-config already applies to the policy dials: a precedence rule lets a half-finished migration keep working while quietly reading the stale half, so the conversion is never finished and nobody finds out. ONE HOME for the vocabulary is an owner ruling (2026-08-10, repo-lock D-6) amending F5: duplicating a vocabulary fails silently - the copy that has not learned a column returns a row missing that cell, which every consumer reads as "the cell is empty".
- **TestRefs**: TC-160
- **Status**: Planned
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-160 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-160
- **Verifies**: SR-147;LLR-166
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Drive the carrier reader over a REAL repo whose spine is cut over from CSV to TOML in one commit, and assert the four properties that fail open if wrong: the pre-migration revision still reads through the carrier it used then; the post-migration revision reads through the new one presenting the same column names; a lossless cutover is SILENT to the amendment guard while text smuggled into that same commit is NAMED with its row and cell; and a carrier that does not parse - including the duplicate id the carrier turns into a decode error - is reported absent rather than empty. Then pin the vocabulary against drift: the reader is the exact inverse of the writer's KEY, and every column of every LIVE spine header is driven through the pair so the agreement cannot be vacuous.

## SR-148 — Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-148 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-148
- **Title**: Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order
- **SN-Refs**: SN-025;SN-029;SN-027;SN-006;SN-008;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall select the work an unattended run does next from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone — shipping no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and deriving from no prose surface and no predefined track — in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, human holds applied only from the declared ratification level, nothing admitted past a human-held stop, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.
- **Rationale**: Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. Absorbs SR-141 (census F5, ruled overlap): both rows stated where adjudication ranks at admission and the acceptance texts had already diverged in nuance — one SR per (SN-025, loop selection order) per 13p; SR-141's stable-partition mechanism lands as an LLR under this row, and its id retires under the D-1/D-4 doctrine with the forwarding record in the log. ABSORBS TWO MORE, 2026-08-15, on the same doctrine and the same invariant: the top-down read found SN-025's loop work-selection stated THREE times — this row, SR-153 ("Ready work derives from tracked state") and SR-059 ("Retire next-wi; generated status surface") — with the ordering claim made twice and "no hand-maintained pointer" three times, against 13p's one-SR-per-(need, property) rule. SR-153 contributed the derivation source (tracked registries and git history alone), the determinism of the eligible set and its order, and the per-item fail-closed rule for missing, undeclared or contradictory declared inputs; SR-059 contributed the no-pointer-surface obligation in its strong form (no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links one) and the generated, freshness-gated status surface. Both ids retire; `sn_refs` unions to SN-025 + SN-029 + SN-027 + SN-006 + SN-008 + SN-010 so no need loses its carrier, and every shed clause is token-checked into this row's requirement or acceptance with the map recorded in the log. The alternative — an explicit three-way partition — was rejected for the reason census F5 gave when the same question was asked of SR-141: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A ready adjudication row admits ahead of every other class, the ruled rank table unchanged (--explain still reports it) and non-adjudication rows keeping their relative order; an unresolved handback is selected before spine or implementation work, and processing it either resolves the record or mints a provenance-linked successor exactly once; with no unresolved handback, only the earliest incomplete spine tier is eligible; implementation work is ineligible while any required test case remains unlaid; at human_ratification_through = 0 no spine tier produces a human hold, levels 1 through 4 hold exactly their documented cumulative tiers, and a human-held attestation still drains and surfaces with the adjudication row waiting behind the stop; every selection records the chosen class, source identifier and hold decision. Two resumes over the same tracked state select the same eligible set in the same order; an item with missing or contradictory declared inputs is excluded with a stated reason while disjoint classified work stays eligible; no prose surface participates in the derivation; the ordering and exclusion reasons are exposed for replay. A fresh scaffold ships no next-wi/run-phase pointer and is parallel-by-default; no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links such a pointer; a build-out lane's phase resolves from its branch name with no pointer file; the status snapshot regenerates mechanically and its freshness is gated.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: unattended-loop
