### LLR LLR-149 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-149
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148
- **Title**: §A8 admission: the kind x ratification-level table + the spine barrier
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _kind_action/_admission/_lane_count/gap_census
- **Detail**: The dispatcher's one scheduling decision (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4/§A8), consuming schedule.kind_of - the §A1 classification's enforcement point. _kind_action is the ruled §A8 table verbatim per kind x human-held answer (`agent_common.human_holds`), and a human-held attestation/gate row SURFACES even under keep_nondependent — that dial answers whether OTHER lanes keep running, never whether the machine may ratify; _admission is the barrier as a pure decision: any exclusive-kind frontier row stops new admission (wait) until every lane is back, then ALL spine rows admit together as one exclusive batch (one branch, one agent_loop --wi 'A;B' worker, one re-attest window), attestation/gate rows surface under attended (drain, exit 0 naming the open-items cards) and admit as the queued batch at single-ratify's close; _lane_count resolves the lanes dial CLI > AGENT_LANES > stack.ini [agent-loop] > 1 (absent key = 1; the template seeds 2); gap_census is the empty-frontier ladder's rung-1 seam - unverified in-scope SRs, orphan rows and draft SNs from trace.analyze, handed to the intake mint helper (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2), never minted in the lane.
- **Rationale**: The §A4.1 authority flip: admission replaced the claim rung's deleted safety_class refusal, so the policy must live at ONE decision point and be driven as a pure function.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-143)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-152 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-152
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148
- **Title**: The adjudication kind: rank-1 slot + no-bar arm + the bar strictness key
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/schedule.py
- **CodeSymbol**: SAFETY_CLASSES/_KIND_CONCURRENCY/_KIND_RANK
- **Detail**: The `adjudication` kind (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2) joins the declared SafetyClass vocabulary: exclusive on the concurrency axis, rank 1 on the order axis - the slot §A1 reserved, filled without renumbering the ruled table. The kind's lane runs NO product bar (integrate._lane_bar_directives + _refresh_bar: the refresh still merges trunk in, runs the trunk step and commits a verified Bar-Green attestation whose summary reads no-bar honestly) because its outputs are Status cells and the work registry, nothing a product bar can speak to; a mixed batch or unreadable frontmatter fails TOWARD the bar. The optional `bar = DevBar-Reqs|DevBar-Tests|DevBar-Release` frontmatter key rides the same directive read: bar declares verification strictness for this row's lane; it never affects scheduling (load_wis deliberately does not parse it) - the refresh passes the batch's strictest value to check.py --gate and refuses a malformed one loudly.
- **Rationale**: Narrowing the amendment detector to ratified cells without the kind would move mis-fires from spurious window to MISSED window; the two are a pair (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A5.2).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-146)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-159 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-159
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148
- **Title**: Admission order and the red-TC census
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _judgement_first/_admission/red_tc_census/parse_red_tc/_implemented_ids
- **Detail**: `_judgement_first` is a STABLE PARTITION over the frontier, applied inside `_admission` before the surface/dispatch split so it can never move work past a human-held stop. `red_tc_census` rides the existing finding-string seam with a distinct prefix; `_red_tc_line` and `parse_red_tc` sit adjacent because the grammar is a contract between two modules, and `intake` routes on the parser rather than re-splitting prose. red_tc_census names, as its own class, a test case that is not green while every requirement it verifies is cited by a work item recorded done — Verified, Draft and Modified test cases are exempt, an open, partial or cancelled citer makes nothing red, and a test case verifying a mix of claimed and unclaimed targets is not red; each named line routes to an adjudication row whose suggested tier is estimated from measured breadth, never defaulted; the census line's grammar has exactly one reader (parse_red_tc).
- **Rationale**: The alternative to the partition — renumbering `schedule._KIND_RANK` — would change ordering for every downstream repo for a rung that belongs to admission. The alternative to the parser is prose carrying control flow, which is the exact defect the typed `suggested_tier` field replaced.
- **TestRefs**: TC-154
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-167 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-167
- **SR-Refs**: SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
- **Title**: The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
- **CodeSymbol**: compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
- **Detail**: The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
- **Rationale**: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).
- **TestRefs**: TC-161
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-161 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-161
- **Verifies**: LLR-167;IF-124;IF-127
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Drive the real loop against a fake agent CLI over throwaway git repos, one per ROUTED brief, and capture what the session was actually handed. Assert the composed brief carries each slot's real derivation (the lane's report verbatim, the closed spec, the commit facts; the live census joined to the TC row and to the obligation it covers), that no `{slot}` survives in the output, and that the typed verdict line the brief demands is written to the path the brief named and committed with the result trailer. Then drive the refusals: a clean close (no per-close report), a report with no typed `commit_range`, and a census that has come clean each fall back to the worker assignment with the reason PRINTED. Pin the discriminator against the ambiguity it exists for - two rows with an IDENTICAL SpecRef and different declared `Brief` cells compose differently - and pin the two unrouted briefs as unrouted, naming themselves in the refusal. The amendment arm covers the two seams cited above (added 2026-08-15, log 2026-08-15h): IF-124, the `last_approved` baseline read - the brief carries the snapshot as its anchor, only RATIFIED cells reach the judge, and with NO snapshot the brief HOLDS and says FIRST APPROVAL rather than fabricating an anchor; and IF-127, the lazily imported re-attestation MODEL, which is what makes the brief and the document the owner signs one traversal rather than two.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-146 AcceptanceCriteria
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_adjudicate_brief.py
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-149 — Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-149 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-149
- **Title**: Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces
- **SN-Refs**: SN-004;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The harness shall report every occurrence of a retired process tag in a live authored surface — naming the file and line and the vocabulary that replaces it, at a severity that warns by default and fails under --strict — reporting none in a declared historical, generated or attestation-quoting surface, nor in a line or file marked as a declaration site.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-004 (the ladder's vocabulary is the one the project is held to) and SN-010 (docs stay honest). OI-21 made this check a CONDITION of the vocabulary conversion rather than a follow-up, on measured evidence: an earlier sweep removed a retired-tag construct from this repo's own open-items registry and it REGENERATED within days, caught only by an audit. A ~2,500-edit conversion held in place by attention is a conversion that comes undone. The carve-outs are as load-bearing as the rule: history is not rewritten and an attestation is never re-worded, because a swept sign-off makes a signed record claim something was signed that was not.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: check_vocab.py reports a planted retired tag in a live authored file with its path and line, exits 0 under the default severity and 1 under --strict; reports nothing for a file under any declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, attestation briefs, closed and cancelled work-item specs, generated surfaces) including at arbitrary depth; reports nothing for a line carrying the allow marker or a file carrying the allow-file marker; and reports nothing for the word "gate" used to mean a check that can fail.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-169 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-169
- **SR-Refs**: SR-149
- **Title**: Retired-vocabulary enforcer
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_vocab.py
- **CodeSymbol**: scan
- **Detail**: Walks the tree, classifies each file through in_scope (declared carve-out globs for historical, generated and attestation-quoting surfaces, plus a text-suffix filter), and reports one finding per offending LINE - not per tag, since a line naming three tags is one sentence to rewrite. Matching is TAG-SCOPED (a whole-word alternation over the retired G tags), never the word 'gate', which survives wherever it means a check that can fail. Declaration sites opt out through an explicit in-file marker rather than a heuristic, because a heuristic that can be satisfied by accident is how the vocabulary grows back through the enforcer meant to stop it. Wired into check.py at EVERY bar with --strict added from DevBar-Tests on, mirroring check_trajectory's severity ladder: the surfaces the tags regrow into are authored hardest at the lowest bar.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-163)
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-163 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-163
- **Verifies**: SR-149;LLR-169
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Plant a retired tag in a live authored file and one in each declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, an attestation brief, a closed work-item spec, a generated surface, the owner scratchpad), plus one at depth under a carved-out directory; run check_vocab.py at both severities; plant a line marker and a file marker; plant the word 'gate' in its surviving sense; and run the checker over the real meta-repo tree.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: The live file is reported with its path and line; the default severity exits 0 and --strict exits 1 on the same finding; no carve-out is ever reported, at any depth; a marked line is skipped while its unmarked neighbour is not, and a marked file is skipped whole; the surviving sense of 'gate' is clean; and the meta-repo itself is clean at the ERROR severity, so the assertions cannot pass against fixtures while the conversion rots.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_stage_ladder.py
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-150 — Stakeholder-need cells stay in stakeholder language

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-150 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-150
- **Title**: Stakeholder-need cells stay in stakeholder language
- **SN-Refs**: SN-033
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The harness shall report each stakeholder-need `need` cell that contains an internal path, an implementation-only identifier or a process citation, naming the row and the offending phrase — at a severity that warns by default and fails only under --strict, exempting a name declared on a reviewed exception list that ships empty (a name that is itself a user-facing interface belongs in a need), and reporting nothing for acceptance or engineering-requirement cells, which SN-033 exempts by its own text.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-033 — its ratified acceptance commissions exactly this check, and until this row it did not exist. Landed while the need tier was measured clean (0 of 27 cells; decision 7 rider 2, ruled 2026-08-13u), deliberately AHEAD of the SR re-tier's churn: locking the clean state in costs one warn-first step now; trusting a large re-statement pass not to dirty it costs an audit later. Warn-first is the ruled posture, not a softness — a form heuristic over ratified stakeholder prose must not gate without an owner ruling, and that ruling has not been made.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: check_need_form.py reports a constructed need cell planted with an internal path, an implementation-only identifier and a process citation, naming the row id and each offending phrase, exiting 0 by default and 1 under --strict; a token on the reviewed exception list is not reported; the same tokens planted in an acceptance or engineering-requirement cell are not reported; and the live registry reports zero findings.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Planned
- **Phase**: 5

### LLR LLR-170 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-170
- **SR-Refs**: SR-150
- **Title**: Need-form checker
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_need_form.py
- **CodeSymbol**: scan/need_findings/load_allow
- **Detail**: Reads the needs registry through spine_carrier.load_needs (either carrier answers; an unreadable registry REFUSES loudly rather than scanning as empty), and a registry that is PRESENT but yields zero scannable need cells - an empty file, or real rows with no need field - is reported VACUOUS rather than clean, while an absent registry stays the pre-scaffold clean skip and a -000-only scaffold registry stays a blank form (REVIEW-A round 1 drove the hole: at DevBar-Reqs an emptied registry hard-failed nothing else in the harness). The three token classes are data (CLASSES), labelled with SN-033's own vocabulary so a finding names the class the acceptance names. Two scope decisions are deliberate and documented in the docstring: SN-### is NOT a process citation (the SN registry is the stakeholder's own document, so a need pointing at a sibling need keeps the reader at the stakeholder tier - the live SN-025 case), and a single-slash, dot-free token is a path only when it RESOLVES in the scanned tree (docs/archive names a real directory and reports; English either/or pairs like subjective/perceptual resolve to nothing and stay exempt - review round 1 narrowed the original blanket exemption). URL spans - scheme:// and scheme-less www. forms alike - are suppressed whole, so a path-shaped tail inside an external address never reports under any class; the span stops at prose delimiters (comma/semicolon) and the www. form requires a multi-label host, so a genuine token abutting a URL and a local www.assets/logo.png both still report (round 3); a sentence-final token's trailing full stop is stripped before the phrase is judged or reported, so its reviewed exception matches (round 2). One finding per token: a later class re-matching inside an already-reported span is the same phrase to rewrite. The exception list (docs/need-form-allow) requires the ' — <reason>' separator per entry, the literal em-dash with spaces (ALLOW_SEP) - a line authored with a plain hyphen, or with no separator at all, declares nothing, so a malformed entry can only ever fail LOUD, never silence a finding. Wired as check.py's need-form step at every bar with NO --strict promotion, deliberately unlike check_vocab's severity ladder: promotion is an owner ruling, and the wiring says so.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-164)
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-164 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-164
- **Verifies**: SR-150;LLR-170
- **Level**: Unit
- **Method**: Construct a needs registry whose need cell plants one token of each declared class (an internal path, an underscore identifier, a registry id and a section mark); run check_need_form.py at both severities; plant a user-facing interface name with and without its reviewed exception-list entry, and with a malformed entry missing the reason separator; plant the same dirty tokens in why/acceptance cells only; plant an English either/or pair, a sibling SN-### citation and a -000 example row; plant a one-level dot-free token both with and without a resolving target in the scanned tree; plant a URL whose tail is path-shaped, in scheme:// and scheme-less www. forms, one sharing its cell with a genuine internal path, one abutting a second path through a comma/semicolon, and a single-label www.-prefixed local path; end a cell with a path token and with an English either/or pair at the full stop; empty the registry file, then strip the need fields from real rows, then leave only a -000 example row; run over a tree with no needs registry at all; and run over the real meta-repo tree.
- **Tier**: Smoke
- **Expected**: The dirty cell reports the row id AND each offending phrase, one finding per token (the path's inner filename is not a second finding), exit 0 by default and 1 under --strict; the exception-list entry silences exactly its token while the malformed entry silences nothing; exempt cells, either/or pairs, SN cross-references and example rows report nothing; a one-level dot-free token reports as a path only when it resolves on disk, and a URL's path-shaped tail never reports in either form while its cell-mate genuine path, a delimiter-abutting neighbour token and a single-label www.-prefixed local path all still do; a sentence-final path is named without its trailing full stop — so its reviewed exception matches — and a sentence-final either/or pair stays exempt; a registry that is present but yields zero scannable need cells reports as vacuous (strict exit 1) while an absent registry is a clean skip and a -000-only registry stays clean; and the live registry is clean at zero findings, so the assertions cannot pass against fixtures while the tier dirties.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_check_need_form.py
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-151 — Hosted CI runs the declared bar per trigger

### SR SR-151 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-151
- **Title**: Hosted CI runs the declared bar per trigger
- **SN-Refs**: SN-005;SN-004
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-06
- **Requirement**: The shipped reference CI workflow shall invoke, for each declared hosted trigger (push, pull request, schedule) across its declared OS x Python matrix, the same documented harness entry point at the tier the declared moment-to-tier table (docs/stack.ini [ci-tiers]) assigns to that moment.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-005 — one definition of passing per moment, its acceptance's own words — and SN-004 at the hosted edge. The obligation existed but had no row of its own: it rode SR-019's acceptance cell (census finding F1), so the B-06 crossing was a frame nobody had stated an observable at. Stated artifact-shaped per §1R.6: the job files are a standing thing whose declarations are pinned by test. An adopter-edited workflow copy is outside this claim, exactly as SN-005 narrows it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: tests/test_ci_tier_declaration.py pins the shipped workflow's trigger set and per-trigger tier against the declared [ci-tiers] table; a workflow trigger absent from the table, or one invoking anything other than the documented harness entry point, is a finding; the declared OS x Python matrix cells appear in the shipped workflow; SR-019's acceptance cross-references this row rather than restating it.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-152 — Hosted CI verdict is the harness's own

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

- **SN-Refs**: SN-008;SN-005
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-07
- **Requirement**: The hosted CI job verdict shall carry the harness's own exit for the declared bar — the job failing when the harness exits nonzero, with a step log naming each step's outcome and no silently skipped required step — as the declared backstop re-run behind the bypassable local hook floor.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a green) and SN-005 at the B-07 crossing. This is the second half of the honest-limit pair the frame's B-04 row states: a local floor is bypassable (git commit --no-verify), so no-unchecked-write-enters-governed-state is validatable only as B-04's verdict at the moment of the act PLUS this hosted re-run of the same bar. SR-114 keeps the portability obligation; the CI matrix as an evidence surface is this row's.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: The shipped workflow contains no continue-on-error or conditional skip around a required harness step; a nonzero harness exit fails the job; the step log names the harness invocation and its tier. This row is the backstop half of the pair SR-019 states from the local side.

## SR-154 — Independent review routed across families

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-154 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-154
- **Title**: Independent review routed across families
- **SN-Refs**: SN-026;SN-024;SN-006
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Before unattended work integrates, the delivered loop content shall obtain each review or critique verdict the declared policy requires from a session that did not author the work — resolved per in-process phase and tier from the delivered agent registry's declared (family x model x tier) rows and only while the declared consent surface that turns managed selection on is present, drawn from a different model family wherever one is configured, degrading only to the documented same-family mode — with every selection logged before launch and a non-converging rework loop escalated through the declared ratification level.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-026 (cross-family second opinions — every prior carrier of this contract demoted as routing machinery, so the sn_refs join forced the mint; SR-155 cites SN-026 too, for the planner pair, but only this row states the second opinion itself), SN-024 (an author cannot judge its own output) and SN-006. One row because routing, scheduling, scoring and escalation are one delivered contract — that the second opinion happens, independently, or its absence is documented — whose mechanics are the component details below it. Extended 2026-08-15: SN-026's DECLARATION and CONSENT half — `docs/agents.toml`'s pair-rows and the `docs/agents-enabled` surface whose presence turns managed selection on — had ZERO textual occurrence anywhere in the SR layer while `orphans=0` still held, because an orphan count proves a need has a citing row and never that the row carries the whole need. Managed selection is opt-in by consent rather than by configuration alone: a repository that declares families without dropping the surface has declared a capability, not turned one on, and the row now says so where a check can read it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A declared review policy of N schedules N independent reviewer sessions; the declared (family x model x tier) pair-rows in docs/agents.toml are the only candidate source and docs/agents-enabled is the consent surface — absent, managed selection is off and no row is resolved; present, exactly one row resolves per in-process phase and tier; a configured cross-family draw is preferred and its unavailability degrades to the documented mode, never a silent skip; every selection is logged before launch, never a silent model swap; rework iterates within the declared budget and pages per the declared ratification level on exhaustion.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: unattended-loop

## SR-155 — Contested planning rounds for marked work

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-155 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-155
- **Title**: Contested planning rounds for marked work
- **SN-Refs**: SN-024;SN-006;SN-026;SN-002
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: For work declared as contested planning, the delivered loop content shall produce the selected decomposition through a bounded round of two independently authored rival plans — cross-critiqued, mechanically coverage-compared and arbitrated by fresh sessions — committing the selected outcome atomically as registry-valid queued rows and surfacing a non-converging round through the declared ratification level, never falling back to a single uncontested plan silently.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-024 (a decomposition judged by a competing plan and an independent arbiter rather than its author), with SN-026 (the planner pair prefers distinct families), SN-002 (the selected outcome lands as registry-valid rows, not prose) and SN-006. One row because the dual-plan round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class (SELECT or PAGE); its state machine, briefs, adapters and isolation rules are the component tier's.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A row marked for contested planning is refused on the direct build path; the round runs planner pair, cross-critique, mechanical coverage comparison and position-swapped arbitration as fresh sessions within declared budgets; SELECT files children that pass the registry validator atomically; PAGE preserves the round's evidence and maps through the declared ratification level.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-156 — Serialized, gated integration of parallel lanes

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-156 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-156
- **Title**: Serialized, gated integration of parallel lanes
- **SN-Refs**: SN-027;SN-008;SN-025;SN-010
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered loop content shall land every lane's finished work through one serial, fail-closed integration seam that runs the declared bar on the composed tree and regenerates the shared authority surfaces only at that seam — draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and recovering from git history alone at any crash boundary — never a half-integrated authoritative state.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-027 — fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed), SN-025 (recovery from git alone) and SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane). One row because serial integration is one delivered contract; the trunk step, pause drain, fragment compilation and verdict-freshness mechanics are its component details.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A finished branch reaches the trunk only through the serial integrator with the declared bar green on the composed tree — a red parks the candidate and stops the queue loudly; log fragments and generated artifacts are compiled and regenerated only by the serial trunk actor and never committed on a work branch; a declared pause stops claiming and drains in-flight lanes to a merged stop; a crash at any lifecycle boundary recovers from git history without double-assignment.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-060 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-060
- **SR-Refs**: SR-148;SR-156
- **Title**: next-wi/run-phase retirement + generated status/run-state
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
- **CodeSymbol**: REGEN_STEPS/regen
- **Detail**: next-wi/run-phase are gone from every surface; the status snapshot is generated by gen_trajectory --status and re-derived by the trunk lane (trunk_step --regen) after each merge - never hand-copied, never written on a work branch. (The run-state generation half retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5.)
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-060)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-124 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-124
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Generated status + run-state
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
- **CodeSymbol**: REGEN_STEPS/regen
- **Detail**: docs/status.md's generated block is maintained by the trunk lane only: trunk_step --regen runs gen_trajectory --status after derive_gate (the gate is its input), and the status-map freshness gate byte-compares it on the trunk while work branches skip (SS5.2). (docs/run-state and its four-valued dispatcher outcome retired with the dispatcher at Phase 5.) The snapshot is an adopted-toolkit output surface (REL-002), not a system-under-development output; a work branch never commits the artifact.
- **Rationale**: Split from the retirement row: removing the old pointer and generating the surfaces that replace it are separable, and the second is what keeps the replacement from rotting the same way.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-137 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-137
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Serial trunk step (log compile + regen)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
- **CodeSymbol**: compile_log/ordered_fragments/rebase_links
- **Detail**: Compiles docs/log.d/*.md into docs/log.md via plan_artifacts.append_log_summary in (git add-time, filename) order - all fragments validated first (name shape, ## heading, reserved section headings refused, an adding commit required - on HEAD, or on MERGE_HEAD during the integrator's merge composition), relative links rebased from docs/log.d/ to docs/, fragments deleted after append, all-or-nothing on any validation error. Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op.
- **Rationale**: One serial actor per §5.5.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-130)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-140 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-140
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Local integrator (claim + queue + audit)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: claim/finished_branches/_verdict_gate/integrate_one/audit
- **Detail**: claim: §2.3 steps 1+2 with the refusal ladder (tracked pause via agent_common.tracked_pause, dirty tree, existing branch, unsafe name, non-ordinary safety_class, off-frontier id) before a git mv + trunk commit + branch cut. finished_branches: a claimed branch whose tip holds no spec under active/<branch>/ (the closing move IS the signal). integrate_one: --no-ff --no-commit merge, trunk_step.py folded into the merge commit so it passes its own floor, check.py --jobs 0 at the declared tier on the composed tree read fail-closed (nonzero exit OR any SKIP line refuses), verdict gate per docs/process.toml [policies] review_rounds (legacy docs/review-policy read through the migration window) using score_reviews.parse_verdict with the verdict's last branch commit required no older than the last non-review non-fragment commit, ff-only trunk advance, branch deleted on green, candidate parked on red. audit: git log --first-parent --no-merges over --since..HEAD; paths outside docs/work/, docs/log.d/, docs/log.md and the stack.ini [generated] set flag the commit. A missing stack.ini or an empty [product] test declaration refuses the merge; a red bar parks the candidate and stops the queue rather than composing past it.
- **Rationale**: One serial actor, one page; every refusal names its reason; the queue stops at the first red rather than composing past it.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-132)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-141 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-141
- **SR-Refs**: SR-006;SR-156
- **Title**: Work-branch lane signal + freshness skip
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _claimed_work_branch/steps
- **Detail**: On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. The claim signal answers in two stages - docs/work/active/<branch>/ on disk (the fast path), else the branch's own committed history (git log -1 -- docs/work/active/<branch>) - so the closing commit that archives the claim, and every commit after it, keep the skip. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
- **Rationale**: A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-134)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-142 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-142
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Trunk-step artifact regen (dependency order)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py
- **CodeSymbol**: regen
- **Detail**: Regenerates arch-map, okf, derived-gate, dashboard, status block and open-items as subprocesses in dependency order (derived-gate before its two consumers), skipping absent artifact families with a notice and stopping loudly at the first failure. Never commits; the caller owns the commit.
- **Rationale**: derive_gate runs before the dashboard/status regens because docs/gate is their input, not their output.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-135)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-144 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-144
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Terminal lane outcomes (handback + quarantine)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
- **CodeSymbol**: close_partial/quarantine/report_path/render_report/_restore/diff_records
- **Detail**: close_partial: commits the lane's work as-is, moves each claimed spec to the TERMINAL partial/ (nothing re-claims it, so nothing strands) and writes ONE IMMUTABLE per-close report under docs/handbacks/ naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split. The report is the close EVENT's identity — immutable so it never moves, unique so a second close is a second document — which is what dissolved the starvation class five mutable-proxy dedup mechanisms died on. A second close is refused rather than overwriting the first, and any refusal restores every report the call wrote (schedule reads queued+blockref as blocked) and the driver cannot claim and return it forever. quarantine: the ruled red arm - reverts the product paths to the merge base and keeps the failing diff as a bar-inert .patch under docs/work/handback/, reading `git diff --name-status -z` as three-field RECORDS so a rename cannot revert the wrong path, checking every git return code, and restoring rather than reporting when a revert step fails. Both leave the branch finished, so integrate.branch_outcomes reads the outcome off the spec's DIRECTORY - complete/=merged, cancelled/=cancelled, any open folder=handback - and every lane ends in a merge.
- **Rationale**: Making the outcome the directory leaves no fourth answer and no state file that could hold one; partial work lands in trunk where a future WI can find it instead of on a branch nobody will look at.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-138)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-145 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-145
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Link-aware spec-move ritual (move + relink indivisible)
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/spec_move.py
- **CodeSymbol**: move_spec/expected_relink/archive_dest
- **Detail**: move_spec moves a registry/spec file and keeps every markdown link in the repo true as ONE indivisible operation: the moved file's own relative targets are re-relativised against its new directory, every inbound link whose target resolves to the vacated path is redirected (link text untouched, #fragments carried, external/protocol-relative/bare-anchor targets left alone, root-relative targets excused only in the rebase half, line endings preserved via newline=''), and every rewritten file is staged with the move so the ritual lands as one changeset. integrate.claim and handback.close_partial call it for the claim and close moves; the CLI performs the worker's terminal close move and the dated docs/archive/specs/ archival. expected_relink is the pure oracle integrate._abandoned_claim compares byte-for-byte to excuse a crashed claim's own relink writes without widening its conviction to any .md edit.
- **Rationale**: A bare move strands both link directions and the break surfaces late, on the composed tree (measured: one probe archival took the broken-link count from 4 to 8 - 3 outbound, 1 inbound). Making move+relink one indivisible operation is a constraint that leaves the two-thirds state unrepresentable, rather than a check that detects it after the parallel work is done.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-139)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-150 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-150
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Lane mechanics: worktree, worker subprocess, §A2 refresh subprocess
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/lane.py
- **CodeSymbol**: ensure_worktree/worker_argv/spawn_worker/run_worker/spawn_refresh
- **Detail**: One lane's mechanics extracted from the dispatcher (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.2): ensure the lane worktree (integrate.lane_worktree, the one home both the worker and the refresh agree on), build the worker argv with the plain launch's session dials forwarded explicitly, launch the worker subprocess (spawn_worker non-blocking for the lane table and dispatch._launch's own default; run_worker its blocking form - a DECLARED SEAM with no in-kit caller, kept for a synchronous single-lane run, and the shape dispatch._launch's worker= injection substitutes), and run the §A2 station refresh as the lane's OWN subprocess (spawn_refresh - the invoker's integrate.py refresh CLI under the harness interpreter) so N lanes' bars overlap instead of queueing at the dispatcher (§A4.3). Reports outcomes, decides none: the tree signal (specs moved out of active/<branch>/) plus exit codes are the whole protocol - no state file, no back-channel.
- **Rationale**: The §A4.2 split keeps the deciding half (dispatch.py) apart from the running half, so at lanes=1 the dispatcher degenerates to the serial loop and the concurrency is a table size, not a second code path.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-144)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-151 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-151
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: Dispatch-lock claim rung + the spine batch claim
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: _dispatch_lock/claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim
- **Detail**: The §A4.1 authority flip's integrator half (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.1): _claim_refusal's safety_class != ordinary arm is DELETED (admission is the dispatcher's decision) and the claim REQUIRES the dispatch lock (agent_common.dispatch_lock_path, out/agent-loop.lock - the lock a live dispatcher holds for its process lifetime), acquired at ONE site (_dispatch_lock, mirroring _slot's one-site discipline for the merge slot's own lock) with the ladder run before it so the clean-trunk rung never refuses over the lock's own file: a hand claim on an idle station works, a hand claim during live lanes is unrepresentable, and the dispatcher's in-process claim passes dispatch_lock_held=True. claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim/_claim_delta are batch-aware: a spine batch is ONE claim commit moving every batched spec (subject joins the ids; the single-WI subject stays byte-identical), and integrate() takes an optional branches= restriction so the dispatcher merges each lane's branch as its own refresh completes.
- **Rationale**: A constraint instead of a check: the state the old refusal guarded against (a claim written beside live lanes) is now unrepresentable, and the batch makes N spine changes cost one re-attest window (§A4).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-145)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-153 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-153
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: The unified trunk-side intake mint + the context block + the ratification-level arms
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/intake.py
- **CodeSymbol**: intake_after_merge/mint_gap_rows/parse_dispositions/next_wi_id/context_block/flip_verified
- **Detail**: The R1/R3 invariant sealed (log.md Decisions 2026-08-01): a WI id is created only by a human trunk commit or this helper - lanes never mint. next_wi_id is max+1 over every spec FILENAME under docs/work/ (active/<branch>/ included). Three triggers, serial by construction: (a) the ratified/routed-cell diff on the merged commit via check_trajectory.staged_spine_amendments -> one adjudication row listing each changed row/cell/before-after (routed traced cells: SN-Refs, Verifies, and LLR SR-Refs per the ruled cell split); (b) a merged spec carrying ## Handback -> the disposition row (outcomes cancel / defer / re-queue with drafted follow-up / surface an open item; NEVER minted for an adjudication row - no recursion, and handback.hand_back refuses the act itself); (c) the dispatcher's gap census -> concrete gap-closure rows, deduped against every existing row. Drafts-not-mints: a merged adjudication row's ## Dispositions fenced-toml drafts mint at ITS merge, validated loudly. Tier signals are measurable (rows touched, gate delta, handback reason class). context_block renders the pure registry joins (cancelled precedent WITH REASONS first, pending OIs, the LLR/TC code map, CMP knowledge packs, IF seams, precedent reviews) - advisory-never-gating, three consumers. flip_verified enacts ruled decision 2: recommend-only under attended, flip under single-ratify/autonomous, unknown levels fail toward recommend. The mint commit mirrors the claim's bookkeeping shape; every derived title is deterministic, so the sweep CLI re-run is idempotent.
- **Rationale**: A detected event must FORCE a row into the registry with nobody watching, and an id two lanes could pick at once must stay unrepresentable (R1).
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-147)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

### LLR LLR-154 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-154
- **SR-Refs**: SR-156
- **Title**: The merge slot's post-merge intake arm
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
- **CodeSymbol**: integrate_one/_lane_bar_directives/_refresh_bar
- **Detail**: integrate_one's post-merge arm is the intake's one honest hook point: the merge has landed, the slot is still held, trunk is serial - intake.intake_after_merge(root, pre-merge head, head, outcomes, branch) mints there as one bookkeeping commit (RULING-6: docs/work/ + the declared generated set), and a mint refusal stops the run loudly while the merge stands (recovery: fix trunk-side, then python intake.py sweep). The refresh's bar directives (_lane_bar_directives, read off the same trunk claim the slot reads) carry the adjudication no-bar arm and the bar strictness pin to _run_bar's --gate.
- **Rationale**: Minting anywhere else re-opens the two-lanes-one-id collision the trunk-only ruling ended; the slot is the only place trunk moves under a held lock.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-148)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-008
- **Phase**: 4

## SR-157 — Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-157 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-157
- **Title**: Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict
- **SN-Refs**: SN-002;SN-012;SN-025
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (queue-overlap visibility). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Deliberate pair (census F6): the PB-format invariant itself is SR-015's; this row's decomposition carries the checker.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Each declared registry rule violation is reported with row-level attribution; the declared strict set exits nonzero at its gate and advisory classes never join the exit code; absent or placeholder-only optional registries and declared opt-outs are vacuous; the declared verification vocabulary (including Critique) is accepted and an unknown value fails. The declaration this is read against is, as the current set: trace.py's `ID_PATTERNS`, `REQUIRED_FIELDS` and `ENUM_FIELDS` (the id shapes, the required cells, and the closed vocabularies -- SR `Verification`, TC `Tier` -- with the IF/CMP entries advisory by their own recorded ruling), the `--strict` / `--strict-integrity` / `--strict-schema` / `--no-placeholders` flag families and the exit contract stated in that module's docstring; trace_text.py's `form_findings` and `provenance_findings` (gating) beside `paraphrase_advisories` and `ac_advisories` (advisory, never the exit code); and check_trajectory.py's work-item rules R-A, R-D, R-E and R-F with `docs/process.toml [checks] trajectory_check` as the declared opt-out. A rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default: an unreported one is a finding against this row, not an amendment to it.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-002 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-002
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Id + registry-CSV-structure + citation integrity
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace.py
- **CodeSymbol**: integrity_findings/structure_findings/triangle_findings
- **Detail**: Duplicate/malformed id detection per ID_PATTERNS, a per-row column-count check over each CSV registry, and SR/LLR citation coherence (triangle_findings); the always-valid --strict-integrity floor. The id vocabulary spans SR/LLR/TC/PB/REPO/MOD/PART/ASSET/CMP/IF (MOD the legacy compatibility id for current REPO rows); a clean registry set exits 0, and orphans never affect the --strict-integrity verdict.
- **Rationale**: These three are wrong the moment they are written rather than at a gate, and each corrupts the join before the orphan rules could notice — so they run on every commit rather than waiting for a gate that would inherit an already-broken registry.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-002)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-133 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-133
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Spine stand-alone rule
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: provenance_findings
- **Detail**: provenance_findings flags a WI id or a process-doc citation across the normative cells of all three spine registries, joining the --strict failure set via exit_code. The normative cells are the SR Title/Requirement/Rationale/AcceptanceCriteria, the LLR Title/Detail/Rationale, and the TC Method/Expected/Parameters; pointer columns are out of scope, and each finding names the registry, row and cell. Not a finding: an artifact path, a rubric, a sibling spine id, and a port-script name — the ten declared external ports (check.py, bootstrap.py, agent_loop.py, check_vendored.py, gen_cases.py, gen_release_checklist.py, subagent_gate.py, run_menu.py, integrate.py, trunk_step.py) — the script-name carve-out reaching no wider than that set.
- **Rationale**: A citation is a pointer a later reader may be unable to follow, and the row's reason travels with it: when the work item is closed and archived, the row keeps its claim and loses its justification, which is the failure this catches. Applied to every spine registry rather than the SR alone, because the layer the rule could not originally see held the largest pocket of violations — a narrower scope would guard the cleanest layer and miss the dirtiest.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-134 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-134
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Requirement form rules
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: form_findings
- **Detail**: form_findings applies six rules — one obligation per requirement, no non-obligation modal, no actorless passive, no unfalsifiable term, no open-ended clause, no obligation keyword in an LLR Detail — skipping Draft rows, and joins the --strict failure set via exit_code. The negative set holds: a Draft row, a zero-obligation row, a project's own declared alternate obligation keyword, and a multi-clause AcceptanceCriteria are not findings — stated keyword-free here deliberately, since the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use.
- **Rationale**: A row carrying two obligations reports neither when one fails, and undecidable wording is signed on impression rather than evidence — that is the failure all six rules exist to prevent. Draft is skipped because a Draft row is exempt from the child-completeness rules elsewhere in this harness and is by convention the state a requirement occupies while still being written, so an unfinished acceptance criterion is expected there rather than defective; flagging it would make the state unusable for the drafting it exists to allow. This cell says "obligation keyword" rather than quoting the keyword itself because the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use, and an exemption a checker cannot see is one an author can reach for.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-135 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-135
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: Paraphrase advisory
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
- **CodeSymbol**: paraphrase_advisories
- **Detail**: paraphrase_advisories compares a child cell's significant words (alphabetic runs of four characters or more, lowercased) against its parent's and reports when the share of the CHILD's words that also appear in the parent exceeds the declared threshold — the child is the denominator, so a long parent cannot dilute a short echoing child. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set. The two watched relationships are an SR Rationale against its own Requirement and an LLR Detail against its parent SR Requirement — the pairs where the parent is unambiguous; each finding emits a stdout WARNING plus a report line with the exit code unchanged.
- **Rationale**: A child that re-words its parent adds a second home for one fact and no decomposition, so a later amendment updates one copy and the two disagree with nothing to report it. The advisory has its own pipe rather than sharing the acceptance-criteria counter because a shared count would report 'ac-advisories' about a finding that is not one, and a reader acts on the counter name.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-160 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-160
- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: The mechanical queue-overlap pre-filter
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
- **CodeSymbol**: queue_conflict_findings/_title_tokens
- **Detail**: Three signals over OPEN rows only — Jaccard over title token sets at a deliberately high bar, shared `SR-Refs`, and a shared anchor-stripped `SpecRef` — each reported once per pair per signal with both ids, in sorted order. Printed on the warn-only line beside backlog staleness; never appended to `errors`. A shared stopword alone is never a signal, and the findings stay out of the exit code even under --strict.
- **Rationale**: A low similarity bar turns a useful warn into noise that gets ignored, which is worse than not having the warn: this rung catches the same job minted twice, not two rows in the same area.
- **TestRefs**: TC-155
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-158 — Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-158 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-158
- **Title**: Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier
- **SN-Refs**: SN-010;SN-008
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall report a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims — a broken intra-repo link, a missing vision declaration, a reference to an absent path or symbol, a stale generated doc, or a declared figure without its provenance — at the declared warn-or-gate severity for each class.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-010 (navigable, honest documentation) and SN-008 (a green never hides a stale claim). One row because the delivered contract is one honesty verdict over the documentation surfaces; the individual checkers and their carve-outs are component details.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A dead intra-repo link, an absent PROJECT-VISION tag, a stale generated doc under --stale, a dangling backticked path or sym: reference, and a declared fig: marker missing cmd/rev each produce a finding at their declared tier; the declared carve-outs (generated blocks, path-ok and fig-ok lines, unmarked figures, URLs and placeholders) stay silent. The tier for each class is read off its declaration site, as the current set: check.py's built-in `doc-navigability` step, which runs check_docs.py from DevBar-Reqs on -- a broken intra-repo link and a missing or repeated PROJECT-VISION tag exit nonzero, orphans warn unless --strict-orphans, and --stale is warn-only; docs/stack.ini's `[step:doc-refs]` and `[step:figures]` rows, which declare `gates = DevBar-Release` for check_doc_refs.py --strict (gating on dangling alone, with untraced counted and never gating) and check_figures.py --strict; and the carve-out surfaces those checkers read -- `docs/orphans-allow`, `docs/declared-absences`, and the path-ok / fig-ok line markers. A class whose severity is stated at none of those sites is undeclared, and this row is unsatisfied until it is declared there.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 1

- **Detail**: Resolves intra-repo links, requires a PROJECT-VISION tag, and checks generated-doc freshness under --stale. A dead intra-repo link or an absent PROJECT-VISION tag fails; a clean doc set passes.

Bound to its input surface: the authored runtime-flow docs are docs/architecture.md's Runtime flows section, whose retirement is ruled — when that surface goes, this check's input class goes with it and the check stands down, an explicit recorded dependency rather than a silent retirement.

### LLR LLR-038 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-038
- **SR-Refs**: SR-158
- **Title**: Path + sym: reference checker
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_doc_refs.py
- **CodeSymbol**: findings_for/load_symbol_oracle/is_path_shaped
- **Detail**: Scans root *.md + docs/**/*.md; a backticked token is path-shaped when it carries a separator plus a known extension or top-level prefix (URLs/globs/placeholders excluded); GENERATED marker blocks and path-ok lines are skipped; sym:<module>.<name> is resolved against the module map parsed from architecture.md (module tail -> public symbols); warn-first, --strict exits 1. With no module-map inventory the symbol tier skips cleanly while the path tier still runs.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-041)
- **Status**: Modified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

### LLR LLR-146 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-146
- **SR-Refs**: SR-158
- **Title**: Declared-figure provenance presence check
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/check_figures.py
- **CodeSymbol**: findings_for/judge_marker/marker_segments
- **Detail**: Scans root *.md + docs/**/*.md (check_doc_refs.doc_files - the same walk, so the two doc checks agree on the surface and generated trees stay skipped) plus docs/stack.ini, with docs/reviews/ records out of scope (a verdict quotes defective markers as evidence); each opt-in fig: marker is judged on its own attributes and must carry a non-empty, wordful cmd= and rev= pair (rev bare or quoted), or a non-empty derived= derivation; a marker is grammar prose that declares nothing only when every attribute value is wholly a placeholder token (angle-bracket-wrapped, or the bare ellipsis - so the convention text ships scaffold-clean); in a judged marker a placeholder-shaped value counts as absent, never as satisfying (an unfilled rev=<revision> names no revision), while a metacharacter inside a longer value is command text and the marker is judged on its attributes; findings name the missing half and quote the line; GENERATED marker blocks and fig-ok lines are exempt; warn-first, --strict exits 1. Presence only, never truth - re-derivation is rung 2, deliberately not built.
- **Rationale**: Would have flagged all three 2026-08-01 false figures; opt-in keeps the claim honest - declared figures carry provenance, not all figures do.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-140)
- **Status**: Verified
- **Component**: CMP-007
- **Phase**: 1

## SR-159 — Declared-architecture connectivity gaps are reported

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-159 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-159
- **Title**: Declared-architecture connectivity gaps are reported
- **SN-Refs**: SN-023;SN-002;SN-012
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall report declared-architecture connectivity gaps — an unresolvable interface row, an undeclared endpoint or direction, a cross-component import no declared interface covers, an invalid spec interface citation, and a top view past its declared bound — warn-first with --strict gating, vacuous where the optional inventories are absent.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-023 (the parts' connections are as much of the truth as the requirement tree, checked mechanically warn-first — the need's own words), SN-002 (interface rows stay resolvable) and SN-012 (opt-out and vacuity keep the layer free for non-users). One row: the delivered contract is one connectivity verdict; the checkers below it are its decomposition.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: Malformed or unresolvable IF rows are findings; undeclared endpoints, uncovered cross-component edges, invalid spec citations and an oversized top view warn plain and fail --strict; declared opt-outs and small or absent inventories are vacuous; declared seams render without changing the verdict. The rules and their opt-outs are read off their declaration sites, as the current set: trace.py for IF/CMP id shape and the off-spine back-link to the spine (`ID_PATTERNS` and the always-on integrity floor, which joins --strict), its `REQUIRED_FIELDS["IF"]` / `["CMP"]` entries and their enums staying advisory by the recorded warn-first sequencing; check_trajectory.py for the connectivity rules -- every arch-map module resolving to at least one IF endpoint (the "connectivity undeclared" warn), a cross-component import no IF row covers, a spec's `## Interfaces` section citing an unresolvable IF-### or neither a seam nor the intra-module escape, and the `TOP_VIEW_MAX` bound of 10 items (warn plain, error under --strict); `docs/process.toml [checks] interfaces_check` and `components_check` as the two declared opt-outs, with a `source`/`sink` token in an IF row's Notes as the per-module honesty valve; and gen_arch_map.py's generated block in docs/architecture.md as the module inventory every one of those rules joins against. A connectivity rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 1
- **Aspect**: connectivity

Malformed or duplicate ids and unresolved SR/Component links are findings; an unmatched ThisProject endpoint stays advisory-only, and an absent interface registry is vacuous.

- **Title**: Cross-component import coverage

## SR-160 — Front-door launchers for the two universal contributor actions

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-160 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-160
- **Title**: Front-door launchers for the two universal contributor actions
- **SN-Refs**: SN-034
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered scaffold shall place both universal contributor actions — preparing the development environment, and resuming the automated development loop — as launchers at the repository root for each supported platform, each starting its action as a single step — locating a working interpreter by RUNNING a candidate and reporting clearly rather than crashing cryptically when none resolves or a Store alias exits nonzero — or documenting the one platform-required step where a single click is impossible.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-034, whose row is the declared ONE home for launcher-class obligations (root visibility, one action per platform, both universal actions). SN-034 had no citing SR — the launcher rows realized SN-001 and left the need an orphan the strict trace names. One row per 13p: (SN-034, scaffold capability). ABSORBS SR-021's LAUNCHER HALF, 2026-08-15: this rationale previously said that the per-launcher robustness obligation REMAINED SR-021's, cross-referenced rather than restated — and a cross-reference to a row that no longer exists is not a home, so what was pointed at is now stated. SR-021 spanned two audiences at once, the git hooks and the root launchers, which is why it SPLIT rather than moved whole: the hook half is machinery under SR-019/SR-020 (LLR-021), and the launcher half is an observable of THIS row, because a launcher that crashes cryptically has not started its action as a single step. The wiring obligation stays SR-113's, which is a live row.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A fresh scaffold and this repository each carry both launcher sets (dev-setup and agent-resume) at the root for Windows, macOS and Linux; each launches its action as a single step on that platform, or documents the single platform-required step (the Linux execute-bit case); with no working python3 each root launcher reports clearly and does not crash cryptically, and with one it runs its action; the adopter-facing guide names the capability and invites reuse.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-161 — Decompositions carry a perspective record

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-161 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-161
- **Title**: Decompositions carry a perspective record
- **SN-Refs**: SN-036
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered planning content shall leave, with every decomposition it produces, a machine-readable record of the declared review perspectives — each perspective's applicability decision and the requirements or explicit no-finding it produced — such that an applicable declared perspective missing from the record is a reportable finding.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-036 — a decomposition produced from one perspective can look complete while omitting security, operations or first-use failures, and the omission is invisible unless the record names what was considered. SN-036 had no citing SR. The declared-perspective inventory is the ruled hats roster; the record proves coverage and provenance while adequacy stays with the independent review, exactly as the need partitions it.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A decomposition round writes the perspective record beside its artifacts; a declared perspective applicable to the decomposition and absent from the record is reported; the record distinguishes not-applicable from considered-with-no-finding; the independent adequacy review reads the record rather than the authoring transcript.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-162 — Requirement boundary references resolve against the declared frame

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-162 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-162
- **Title**: Requirement boundary references resolve against the declared frame
- **SN-Refs**: SN-037
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered harness shall resolve every system-requirement boundary reference, and every declared interface's endpoint pair and signal type, against the declared external frame — an unresolvable crossing failing the strict trace naming the row — reporting requirements naming no crossing, crossings named by no requirement, declared crossings with no realizing interface row, an interface declaring no endpoint pair or no signal type from the closed discrete/variable vocabulary, and a seam joined to a counterpart whose signal type is incompatible with its own, each as an advisory, and staying vacuous for a project declaring no frame.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-037 — every promised behavior's entry and exit locatable at a declared boundary, unresolved references mechanical findings, in the need's own words. SN-037 had no citing SR even though the machinery is already delivered (the frame registry, the boundary-reference field, the resolution and coverage rules); this row is the obligation that machinery enforces. Severity split deliberately: resolution hard, coverage advisory — the ruled warn-first sequencing while the corpus converges. Extended 2026-08-15: SN-037's SIGNAL-TYPING half — "every referenced interface identifies its boundary endpoints and whether its signal is discrete or variable ... incompatible signal types are mechanical findings" — had ZERO textual occurrence in the SR layer while `orphans=0` held, even though `interfaces.toml` already carries a `signal` cell on every row (many of them typed `variable` by a recorded default with a re-type note, which is exactly the state a check is owed for). NAMED RESIDUAL, stated rather than quietly dropped: the need's last clause — that a reviewed change altering one side of the requirement/interface relationship carries or justifies the corresponding change on the other — is a REVIEW obligation this row does not claim to mechanize, and no SR states it yet.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A Boundary-Refs value naming an undeclared crossing joins the --strict failure set naming the row; the uncovered-requirement count, the crossings named by no requirement, and the realization gap are each one advisory line; an interface row carrying no endpoint pair, or a signal value outside the closed discrete/variable vocabulary, is reported naming the row; two joined seams whose declared signal types are incompatible are reported naming both rows; entity and relationship references in the frame registry resolve; a project declaring no frame is vacuous.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-163 — Every shipped file maps to a stakeholder outcome

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-163 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-163
- **Title**: Every shipped file maps to a stakeholder outcome
- **SN-Refs**: SN-038
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: Every file the delivered package ships shall map, through the declared shipped-file inventory and its recorded exclusions, to at least one system requirement whose references resolve to a stakeholder need — generated outputs mapping through their generator — with missing files, stale inventory entries, unresolved references and unmapped files reported under the declared warning-to-gating policy.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-038 — a supplied file with no recorded purpose is inherited scope creep, and its absence of purpose must be a named coverage finding rather than hidden in the tree. SN-038 had no citing SR. The scaffold MAPPING is the natural declared inventory: it already enumerates what ships, so the coverage universe is a declaration that exists rather than a second list that drifts.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A declared inventory defines the universe and exclusions; a shipped file absent from it, a stale entry naming a file that no longer ships, an entry whose requirement reference does not resolve to a need, and a file with no mapping are each reported; the declared policy assigns warn versus gate per class; a generated output maps through its generator's row.
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

SN-039 had no citing SR. The field enters the SN carrier schema in the scheduled schema batch, per the need's own acceptance, rather than as a one-off column.
