## SR-165 — The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-165 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-165
- **Title**: The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record
- **SN-Refs**: SN-040
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered planning content shall leave, with every component partition it produces, a machine-readable derivation record in the tracked component registry rather than session prose — the candidate partitions considered, the declared objective and constraints they were scored against, each candidate's score, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it — such that a live component row whose record omits any of those elements, or a selection the recorded scores do not rank first without a recorded human override, is a reportable finding.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-040 — a free-form partition leaves no record of the alternatives weighed, so the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents; the record is what lets the choice be re-examined. SN-040 had no citing SR, even as the P5 partition work performed exactly this record. Reproducibility is stated as a recomputation from the record's own declared inputs, not as a reviewer re-running a model session, because only the former can fail — the SR-161 form applied to the partition instead of the perspective set. B-05 alone: the human ruling is an element the record must carry, not an observable the system states at the authority input, which is SR-139's and SR-140's ground.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A partition round writes the derivation record into the tracked component registry, never into session prose or a plan document alone; a live component row with no record, or a record omitting the candidate partitions, the declared objective, the constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition or the human ruling, is reported naming the missing element; a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported unless the record carries the human override that chose it; the recorded ranking recomputes from the record's own objective, constraints and scores alone.
- **Priority**: S
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5
- **Aspect**: process

### LLR LLR-172 (current)
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-172
- **SR-Refs**: SR-165
- **Title**: Component derivation record: element completeness + the ranking recompute
- **Module**: project-trajectory/scripts/trace.py
- **CodeSymbol**: component_findings
- **Detail**: Extends the existing component pass — the one home that already reads docs/requirements/components.toml and already emits CMP findings — with the derivation-record rules SR-165 states, rather than adding a second reader of the same registry. Per live CMP row: the record must declare the candidate partitions considered, the objective and the constraints they were scored against, a score per candidate, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it; a missing element is one finding naming the row AND the element. Two derived rules on top of presence, because presence alone is satisfiable by a record that says nothing: (1) THE RANKING RECOMPUTES — re-rank the recorded candidates from the record's own objective, constraints and scores and compare with the recorded order, a mismatch being a finding, which is what makes the record falsifiable without a reviewer re-running a model session; (2) A SELECTION THE SCORES DO NOT RANK FIRST is a finding unless the record carries a human override naming that choice, so a legitimate "the scores said A, the human chose B" is expressible and an unexplained one is not. Vacuous by construction on a project declaring no components, and on the example -000 rows. NOT BUILT YET: the record cells do not exist on the CMP schema today, which is why this row and TC-166 are Draft — SR-165 was flipped Inspection -> Test at act 7 precisely because the check is mechanizable, and this is the debt that flip incurred, recorded rather than silently owed.
- **Rationale**: The check belongs where component_findings already is for the reason the census keeps applying: a second module reading components.toml would be a second answer to "is this partition recorded", and the two would drift. The recompute is the load-bearing half — an element-presence check passes on a record whose scores contradict its own conclusion, which is the exact failure SN-040 exists to catch (the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents). The override arm is what stops the recompute rule from forbidding human judgement: the requirement is that a departure from the scores is RECORDED, never that it is disallowed.
- **TestRefs**: TC-166
- **Status**: Draft
- **Component**: CMP-006
- **Phase**: 5

### TC TC-166 (current)
- **TC-ID**: TC-166
- **Verifies**: SR-165;LLR-172
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run trace.py's component pass over fixture component registries: (a) a complete derivation record passes silently; (b) a record omitting each element in turn — candidate partitions, objective, constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition, the human ruling — is reported once per omission, naming the row and the missing element; (c) a record whose recorded ranking does not recompute from its own objective, constraints and scores is reported; (d) a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported, and the SAME record with a recorded human override naming that choice is silent; (e) a project declaring no components, and the shipped example -000 rows, are vacuous.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Satisfies SR-165 AcceptanceCriteria via the LLR-172 detail contract
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Evidence**: tests/test_components_registry.py (to be extended at implementation)
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5

## SR-166 — The delivered package is complete and consumable at its declared manifest

_No approved baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval; current state only._

### SR SR-166 (current)
- **SR-ID**: SR-166
- **Title**: The delivered package is complete and consumable at its declared manifest
- **SN-Refs**: SN-001;SN-007
- **Boundary-Refs**: B-05
- **Requirement**: The delivered package shall be complete and consumable against its own declared shipped-file manifest — every file the manifest names present in the package and materialized at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, every file the package ships named by the manifest, and this repository's own instance carrying the structure of the template it ships — a named source that is absent, a declared destination a fresh scaffold does not carry, a shipped file the manifest does not name, and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's instance each failing rather than degrading the delivered package silently.
- **Rationale**: Realizes SN-001 (an adopting team gets a working, gated, requirement-traced process without hand-building the tooling — which presupposes that what arrives is whole) and SN-007 (the kit is held to the standard it ships). §1R.6 names this as B-05's own artifact-shaped observable — the package "exists, is complete, and is consumable" — and records that its manifest is already pinned; the 2026-08 re-tier campaign minted fifteen rows and not this one, leaving the crossing the frame describes at greatest length with no requirement stating it, and MAPPING surviving only in SR-163's rationale. Verification evidence is named rather than owed: tests/test_bootstrap.py drives bootstrap.py's MAPPING against the file lists a fresh scaffold must carry, and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py pins this repository's own instance against the template it ships, so the manifest cannot declare one thing and the package deliver another. Deliberately distinct from its two neighbours: SR-163 asks why each shipped file EXISTS (SN-038, purpose coverage), SR-010 asks whether the generated scaffold runs GREEN, and this row asks whether what was promised is what arrives.
- **AcceptanceCriteria**: A manifest entry whose source the package does not carry fails naming the file; a manifest entry whose destination a fresh scaffold does not contain fails naming the destination; a file the package ships that the manifest does not name fails naming the file; and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's own instance fails naming the surface. Evidence: tests/test_bootstrap.py (manifest against the fresh-scaffold file lists) and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py (shipped template against this repository's instance).
- **Priority**: M
- **Verification**: Test
- **Status**: Draft
- **Phase**: 5
